# ICDT Paper Narrative, Current Evidence, and Next Experiment Plan

Updated: 2026-11-05 (America/New_York). The November 4 version established the paper narrative; Section 6 was added on November 5 to specify subtask construction, comparison design, and the submission schedule. This section governs the current research narrative, interpretation of evidence, and work order. Historical entries below retain their original facts, decisions, and SHAs; their references to “current,” “not yet launched,” and the GPU1 queue no longer describe the present state. Historical validity follows each experiment's frozen protocol and validity record, without reassessment merely because the narrative changed.

The current manuscript is [`latex/latex_icdt`](../../latex/latex_icdt/), organized after the [Introduction in `latex/latex_mga`](../../latex/latex_mga/sections/intro.tex) and its [Experimental Analysis](../../latex/latex_mga/sections/exp.tex). [`latex/cross_ebdm`](../../latex/cross_ebdm/) provides earlier design context. Run logs, experiment decisions, phase names, and task lists belong in this development document; the paper remains an academic draft. This round changes only the manuscript and documentation, without new training, simulation, or GPU jobs. Subsequent computation remains GPU0-only.

The [paper PDF](../../latex/latex_icdt/main.pdf) now synchronizes the abstract, Introduction, related work, method and conditional theory, experimental design, actual results table, and conclusion. The main text ends on page 8; references bring the total to 9 pages. The [layout check](../../latex/latex_icdt/notes/layout_check.json) passes, with no overflow, unresolved references, or bibliography errors. New subtasks and unfinished ablations are explicitly planned work; intended contributions are not presented as completed experimental findings. Compilation uses a temporary directory, retaining only deliverables such as the PDF and check report in the repository.

## Current Completion Status and Evidence Boundaries

The independent GPU0 formal v2 batch completed **36 method–task cells, 232 actor jobs, and 11,600 physical outcomes**. Its execution state is `FORMAL_TABLE_ASSEMBLED`; the main table records `formal_performance_evidence=true` and `all_cells_cpu_reaudited=true`. Independent per-actor audits and CPU table assembly are complete; descriptive uncertainty uses 10,000 resamples. This v2 remains a valid frozen experiment, is not relabeled invalid, and is not pooled with the old GPU1 v1.

- The [formal main table](../../results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/independent_main_table_formal_v2/main_table.json), [uncertainty](../../results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/independent_main_table_formal_v2/uncertainty.json), [statistical protocol](../../results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/independent_main_table_formal_v2/protocol.json), and [execution state](../../results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/independent_main_table_execution_v2/state.json) record the final state.
- The [v2 launch receipt](../../results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/independent_main_table_execution_v2/launch_receipt.json) freezes 1,703 source/task-configuration hashes. The [invalidation record](../../results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/independent_main_table_execution_v1/invalidation.json) for the old GPU1 v1 remains separate.
- Each fixed-arm actor receives 5 physical-reset slots × 2 modes × 4 mechanism groups (`source`, `nominal`, `latency_gain_shift`, `payload_shift`) = 40 requests; the mobile body additionally has 2 support conditions, giving 80. Trainable methods have 8 instances; each of the two fixed public references has one identity. All fixed-arm requests use xArm7, and mobile requests use Go2+Z1. Here `source` means a source-like mechanism on the target body, not Panda source qualification. These are pooled results for this target execution suite, not “success only under unknown target shifts.”
- Subsequent changes to training, actors, tasks, bodies, metrics, or test distributions define new development versions and independently frozen cohorts. Already inspected v2 results may motivate hypotheses but cannot become unvisited test data for those hypotheses.

## 1. Paper Thesis and Introduction Logic

> Existing source skills contain useful interaction structure, but different bodies may not realize it using identical commands. ICDT calibrates execution responses through limited target interventions, transfers the structure that remains useful, and permits deviation when source behavior conflicts with the target task.

Following MGA, introduce a sequence of technical gaps and connect the method, contributions, RQs, and experiments to each. The main table measures the full system; a few ablations and event plots explain why it behaves as observed. Winning every cell is unnecessary; gains must be reproducible, interpretable, and have clear resource costs under prespecified important conditions. Counterexamples should narrow claims or motivate mechanism changes, not post hoc task selection, failure removal, or metric changes.

The Introduction has six paragraphs:

1. **Need for skill reuse.** The source robot already knows detouring, surface following, alignment, and recovery. Transferring to another arm or mobile manipulator should reuse these skills instead of collecting an entire target demonstration set again. Body, control latency, payload, and contact compliance change how identical commands execute.
2. **Recent approaches and their value.** Human2Sim2Robot and Play2Perfect emphasize source behavior, object semantics, and precision-assembly priors; ASID, AdaptSim, and FADA calibrate mechanisms or execution modules using target information; TactAlign and KITE emphasize contact feedback and cross-body interaction representations. Together they show the value of source structure and the need to handle target execution differences.
3. **Specific gap.** Do not claim that all prior methods transfer only actions and ignore contact. The question is narrower: with limited target data, which source interaction responses should be retained, and how can they become executable, task-completing behavior on the target body? Regions where source responses help must be distinguished from regions where the source strategy is incompatible.
4. **Running assembly example.** Both bodies can reach an entrance, but the same lateral adjustment may release a jam on one body and produce a force spike on the other. Improved model prediction alone does not determine whether source recovery direction, speed, and timing should be retained. Target workspace or support constraints may require principled deviation.
5. **ICDT method.** Short interventions provide task-relevant execution information; the calibrated model supports source-conditioned transport. Target task loss remains active, while source-response constraints apply selectively where task compatibility and model support hold. Existing body-control interfaces provide closed-loop execution.
6. **Three contributions and their evidence.** Two methodological contributions and one system-validation contribution. Standard identification, ordinary residual networks, public decoders/WBC, and iterative networks without demonstrated benefits are not separate original contributions.

Proposed title: **ICDT: Intervention-Calibrated Distribution Transport for Cross-Embodiment Contact Manipulation**. Whether “Sim-to-Real” appears in the final title depends on real-robot evidence; simulation does not substitute for hardware results.

| Contribution | Proposed claim | Required evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **C1: Skill transfer through realized execution responses** | Connect source-conditioned skill distributions, target commands, and target closed-loop responses, transferring task-relevant interaction structure through a target-executable mapping | Effective command and response changes, preservation of mode semantics, and execution of the same semantics on different bodies |
| **C2: Intervention-calibrated selective response constraints** | Calibrate task-relevant responses with limited target interaction, retain source structure where valid, and relax constraints under task conflict or insufficient support | Controlled calibration, response, and selectivity ablations; budget-dependent and event-conditioned mechanism evidence |
| **C3: Performance and resource validation across increasing interaction complexity** | Establish the full method's value and limits under constrained geometry, sustained contact, hybrid contact recovery, and body-support coupling | Strong-baseline main table, target-access budgets, end-to-end execution cost, and actual cross-DOF/mobile-body results |

These are contributions to establish. Current evidence does not demonstrate overall superiority over ASID/AdaptSim or a stable transport-only increment on critical-contact tasks. Research objectives must not be stated as achieved findings.

### Related Directions and Baseline Roles

| Approach/work | Narrative and comparison role | Current treatment |
|---|---|---|
| [Human2Sim2Robot](https://human2sim2robot.github.io/), [Play2Perfect](https://play2perfect.github.io/) (work associated with Karen Liu) | Object semantics, source-behavior priors, and precision assembly | H2S2R remains outside the main table. Play2Perfect's dexterous-hand play prior differs from the fixed-grasp setting; it is a candidate for a targeted comparison, not an automatic main-table entry |
| [FADA](https://github.com/LeCAR-Lab/FADA), [DIAL-MPC](https://github.com/LeCAR-Lab/dial-mpc) (work associated with Guanya Shi) | Execution adaptation and online model optimization | Retain in the core table, explicitly distinguishing repository adaptations from original systems |
| [ASID](https://weirdlabuw.github.io/asid/), [AdaptSim](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v229/ren23b.html) | Learning after active identification and task-driven simulator adaptation | Competitive current results justify retaining both as strong references; these are respectively 2024 and 2023 works, not collectively “methods from the last two years” |
| [TactAlign](https://www.roboticsproceedings.org/rss22/p006.html), [KITE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22113) | Contact semantics and cross-body separation of intent and execution | Position the gap precisely; do not promise additional formal rows before verifying code, interface, and data reproducibility |

The paper cannot position itself by comparing only with old methods. Recent work establishes existing capabilities and remaining gaps; main-table methods also require problem fit, executable implementations, and clear data permissions. ACP/Human2Sim2Robot remain outside the main table as already decided by the user. Do not tune indefinitely when source learning is unestablished, or score missing execution as zero success.

## 2. Three Research Questions and Increasing-Complexity Experiments

| RQ | Contributions addressed | Main environments/evidence | Key comparisons |
|---|---|---|---|
| **RQ1: Can limited target interaction provide sufficient execution information to transfer source skills?** | C1, C2 | Constrained transport and basic guided alignment; response prediction and constrained success versus target budget | ASID, AdaptSim, FADA, and no-calibration ablation; complete collection/model-selection/adaptation ledger |
| **RQ2: As contact persistence, switching, and environment dynamics increase, when should source responses be retained or departed from?** | C2 | Curved guidance, jam recovery, compliant fixtures; contact events, peak force, and recovery | No-response and nonselective constraints, plus the strongest task baseline |
| **RQ3: Can identical task semantics transfer across degrees of freedom and support morphology at reasonable execution cost?** | C1, C3 | Panda→UR5e; fixed arm→Go2+Z1; crossed body/mechanism changes | Direct source transfer, ASID, AdaptSim, DIAL, public feedback; end-to-end latency and body constraints |

### Four Environment Families: First Complete Two Core Subtasks per Family

Increase complexity through new task relationships, not by calling two friction coefficients two tasks. Each second core subtask requires separately frozen feasibility, success criteria, and source skills. “Planned” means complete results do not yet exist. First establish **8 core subtasks**; add third subtasks only when mechanism evidence remains incomplete.

| Environment family/current configuration semantics | Core subtask 1 | Core subtask 2 | Optional third subtask |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Constrained-space held-object transport** `obstacle_transfer` | Dual-route obstacle avoidance and orientation-constrained placement (existing foundation) | Narrow-aperture passage followed by short-distance braking and placement (planned; orientation and velocity timing) | Consecutive differently oriented apertures with intermediate reorientation |
| **Surface-guided contact alignment** `guided_contact_alignment` | Dual-channel contact-guided alignment (existing strict contract) | Advance along a cornered or curved guide to a stop (planned; sustained contact and direction switching) | Edge sliding followed by pivot-based rotational alignment |
| **Connector assembly with recovery** `keyed_insertion_recovery`/`compliant_keyed_insertion_recovery` | Offset insertion, jam withdrawal, and realignment (existing task) | Insertion into a single-axis compliant fixture (development physics and prediction data exist; policy experiments pending) | Small-angle locking after insertion, adding a contact phase |
| **Body-support-coupled assembly** `mobile_keyed_insertion_recovery` | Same insertion from a fixed stance (existing foundation) | Reposition within the support region, stabilize, then dock (planned; explicit body coordination) | Recover docking alignment after a disturbance during holding |

Apply these principles when freezing:

- Latency, gain, payload, friction, and fixture stiffness are within-subtask mechanism-generalization axes. Separate training, development, and held-out combinations explicitly; cross body and mechanism changes to avoid confounding them.
- New tasks require corresponding source skills; for example, an old static-insertion prior cannot be assumed to cover locking. Cross-body comparisons use the same task's source prior and declare the target execution interface and accessible state.
- Main comparisons use targets jointly reachable by both bodies under physical constraints. Source modes infeasible on the target form a separate diagnostic for selective deviation; the main table should not predominantly measure unreachable resets.
- The current guided-contact contract checks contact at 1 ms physics substeps: at least 50% coverage, no uninterrupted detachment exceeding 150 ms, through the requested mode exit. Brief detachments are allowed. If new curved tasks require continuous contact, define a new contract instead of retroactively changing old success criteria.
- The current fixed-arm main-table target is xArm7; there are no formal Panda→UR5e results. UR5e is new cross-DOF evidence for RQ3. Current Go2+Z1 results establish stance manipulation/support, not gait, navigation, or real mobile manipulation.
- v2 pools target-body source-like, nominal, and shifted groups. Future protocols must record every request's body/mechanism identity, separately report target nominal/shifted conditions, and retain target source-like diagnostics. Actual source-robot qualification is separate. Preserve complete stratified tables; do not recompute the old table and relabel it as the original preregistered primary metric.

### Nine Main-Table Rows and Three Principal Ablations

| Main-table row | Role |
|---|---|
| ICDT | Complete method |
| Task-only | Capacity-matched internal reference; may remain in the general table but is not external SOTA |
| FADA | Recent reference with frozen behavior planner and adapted execution module |
| DIAL-MPC | Online iterative model-optimization reference |
| Response MPC | Conventional model-optimization reference |
| Public geometry/feedback | Tests whether public feedback already solves the task |
| Public source prior | Direct source-transfer reference |
| AdaptSim | Task-driven simulator adaptation |
| ASID | Active system identification followed by task learning |

Every main-table method participates in all core subtasks under identical task-success/constraint contracts. Methods may retain reasonable native control structures; system comparisons need not force all methods into one algorithm. Missing implementations are “not executed”; preserve actual execution failures without conflating the two as zero success. Document each FADA, DIAL, ASID, and AdaptSim adaptation, original algorithmic core, execution change, and training permission.

Use the common objective `J = E_i[C_i + β h_i + λ sg(G_i) R_i + Ω_i]`, where `C_i` is task loss, `h_i` is the task-band hinge relative to frozen task-only, `R_i` is the source-response constraint, `G_i` is a selective gate recomputed each update with stopped gradients, and `Ω_i` contains common regularization/control constraints. The table omits sample indices and expectation; `h` denotes the same hinge. Highlight three ablations:

| Ablation | Main change from the full method | Question addressed |
|---|---|---|
| **No target calibration** | Use nominal context/model; match other training and execution conditions | Does limited target intervention provide useful execution information? |
| **No source-response constraint** | `C + β h + Ω`, retaining task-band | Does the source-response term provide an independent task increment? |
| **No selective constraint** | `C + β h + λ R + Ω`, removing gate-based selection of the source-response term | When does forced source-response preservation introduce bias? |

Current pure task-only `C` differs from the full method in both response and band terms, so it cannot isolate the response effect. The three main ablations match warm start, data stream, optimization steps, action permissions, executor, selector, mode policy, and model-selection budget. Necessary changes must be labeled system comparisons rather than single-factor attribution. Keep pure task-only and place the older six-cell objective decomposition in the appendix. MLP/four-step iterative parameterizations also belong there; near-saturation in four cells does not make diffusion a central contribution.

### Four General-Table Metrics

| Metric | Definition and purpose |
|---|---|
| **Constrained success ↑** | Task completion while satisfying collision, contact, force, and body-support contracts |
| **Physical-constraint violation rate ↓** | Fraction of episodes violating any contractual constraint, separating safety failures from ordinary incompletion |
| **Failure-penalized normalized completion time ↓** | Completion time/task limit for successes; 1 for failures; all requests remain in the denominator |
| **End-to-end p95 control latency ↓** | Actual chain including state processing, policy/model, online candidate selection, decoder/WBC; fixed hardware, synchronization, and timing boundaries |

Report by family, weighting subtasks equally within each family and aggregating according to prefrozen rules. Report primary manipulation aggregation separately from mobile-body extension, retaining the scientific roles of “first three manipulation families/fourth morphology extension” so mobile performance does not hide manipulation regressions. Any future aggregation change must be predefined and cannot rewrite v2's original statistical contract.

Put complete subtask results in supplementary tables; budgets, worst-group success, mode correspondence, recovery, peak force, and model error belong in RQ figures. Current mobile worst-group success is zero and must remain visible. Replacing the future fourth main metric with latency does not remove this failure. v2's fourth metric is worst-group success; **a consistently defined nine-method end-to-end p95 table does not yet exist** and cannot be fabricated from single network forwards or incompatible local actor timings.

Candidate target-interaction budgets are 0/30/60/120 seconds, to be frozen before experiments. Allow ASID/AdaptSim and other active methods to choose how to spend their budgets instead of requiring identical two-second probes. Budget accounting includes source data, target calibration, candidate selection, simulated targets, deployment targets, and model-selection access. Sum physical target interaction across the full workflow, not only final deployment. Retain each method's permitted inputs and costs and design common-budget curves. Unequal budgets do not automatically invalidate original experiments, but the current table is not an equal-budget ranking.

## 3. Existing Results: Supported Stories and Missing Evidence

### Constrained Success in Completed v2

Values are percentages pooled over four frozen mechanisms on the target body, not shifted-target groups alone. The `source` mechanism label and historical task IDs precisely identify existing evidence. Fixed references have one actor per task, not eight fictitious training instances. † denotes the repository's task adaptation, which is not unconditionally equivalent to the original paper's complete system.

| Method | Constrained transport | Guided alignment | Static insertion recovery | Mobile-supported insertion | First-three mean |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **ICDT** | 100.00 | 98.75 | 42.19 | 86.88 | 80.31 |
| Task-only | 100.00 | 97.50 | 47.19 | 60.16 | 81.56 |
| FADA† | 0.00 | 0.31 | 0.00 | 0.00 | 0.10 |
| DIAL-MPC† | 100.00 | 0.00 | 36.25 | 83.75 | 45.42 |
| Response MPC | 100.00 | 99.69 | 48.44 | 70.78 | 82.71 |
| Public geometry/feedback | 100.00 | 97.50 | 50.00 | 87.50 | 82.50 |
| Public source prior | 0.00 | 0.00 | 62.50 | 87.50 | 20.83 |
| AdaptSim† | 98.44 | 99.06 | 62.81 | 86.72 | 86.77 |
| ASID† | 96.88 | 98.44 | 59.69 | 87.50 | 85.00 |

Descriptive intervals resample independent training actors and shared physical-reset blocks within frozen mechanism/support groups, keeping modes paired and never duplicating fixed actors as independent ones. All differences below are **ICDT minus comparator success in percentage points**. The 95% intervals are conditional on the frozen mechanisms, support conditions, and target simulator, not guarantees for arbitrary unseen robots; no family-wise multiple-comparison correction was applied.

| Comparison | Difference (pp) | Descriptive 95% interval (pp) | Current interpretation |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Guided alignment − task-only | +1.2500 | [−2.1875, +5.0000] | High success, no stable increment established |
| Static insertion − task-only | −5.0000 | [−18.1250, +8.4375] | Point-estimate regression, interval crosses zero |
| Mobile insertion − task-only | +26.7188 | [+21.5625, +31.4063] | Clear full-system difference, not isolated transport attribution |
| Static insertion − AdaptSim | −20.6250 | [−33.1250, −7.1875] | Strong current baseline leads on this task |
| Static insertion − ASID | −17.5000 | [−30.9375, −3.7500] | Manipulation-performance gap also remains |
| First-three mean − task-only | −1.2500 | [−5.7292, +3.5417] | No support for an overall transport increment |
| First-three mean − AdaptSim | −6.4583 | [−10.7292, −1.7708] | No support for overall full-method superiority |
| First-three mean − ASID | −4.6875 | [−9.3750, +0.1042] | Superiority not established |

| Observation | Supported statement | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|
| Several methods reach 100% on constrained transport | Basic transfer across interfaces is executable | Improve discrimination through meaningful geometry/timing relationships, not merely random noise |
| Guided alignment approaches ceiling | High success under a strict contract | Isolate response effects under matched action permissions; current difference interval crosses zero |
| Static insertion: ICDT succeeds in 135/320, with 185/320 over-force failures | Exposes hybrid-contact and mode-recovery weaknesses | Prioritize early force events, mode policy, action projection, and model-validity horizons; simply adding seeds is insufficient |
| Mobile insertion: ICDT 556/640, task-only 385/640 | Full system runs on this body and outperforms this internal reference | No advantage over ASID/public feedback; retain 80 joint-limit failures, 4 timeouts, and worst-group zero |
| Better short-horizon compliant-fixture holdout prediction | Calibrated model has local predictive value on new resets | Closed-loop task gains, long-horizon prediction, and event safety remain unestablished |
| Parameterization four-cell results: 63/64, 62/64, 64/64, 64/64 | Both parameterizations execute | No robust diffusion or anchoring advantage established |

Preserve these attribution limits:

1. Current insertion/mobile fidelity records set `single_factor_transport_attribution:false`. Full actors include mode selection, projection, or model-selection logic; their complete difference is not the causal contribution of the source-response term.
2. FADA's low scores accompany unestablished learned closed-loop competence in new source/nominal qualification. Local collection/training does not equal the authors' complete oracle PPO + DAgger chain. Evidence of a frozen planner/adapted IDM is valid, but zero scores do not establish an inherent failure of the original method. Distinguish source teacher from learned source.
3. Exploration and selection costs differ across current AdaptSim/ASID and other methods. The current table measures complete systems, not efficiency under a unified full target budget.
4. Fixed-arm results use xArm7; these trajectories cannot be labeled UR. Go2+Z1 results support stance manipulation and support extension, not dynamic walking or hardware success.
5. In the [independent compliant-fixture prediction report](../../results/arm/compliant_keyed_insertion_recovery/validation/fixture_response_model_holdout_20261031/report.json), 8 new public episodes provide 3,623 windows. Overall position MAE at 3/6/10 ticks changes from v2→v3 as 7.72→4.47, 9.16→9.02, and 13.79→13.81 μm; event-window errors are 24.30→23.64, 40.26→37.46, and 50.63→49.38 μm. Gains are primarily short-horizon; average error does not imply contact safety or task improvement. Prioritize contact establishment, force peaks, withdrawal, readvance, and completion events.
6. The existing four-step deterministic DDIM-style branch directly optimizes the ICDT objective without score-matching pretraining. It is an iterative transport parameterization, not a diffusion policy with a learned probabilistic score. Ceiling-level 64/64 development results do not establish distribution-modeling superiority.

The intended evidence structure is competitive simple-task performance; reproducible, interpretable gains in prespecified important contact conditions; realization of the same skill semantics on new bodies; and additional value in data or execution cost. A complete valid main table already exists, but the central mechanism and performance evidence still requires research. Do not preemptively claim broad superiority over strong baselines.

## 4. Theory Adjustments Connecting Mathematics to the Three Contributions

The [ICDT method draft](../../latex/latex_icdt/sections/method.tex) now includes two basic lemmas and a proved conditional event-preservation proposition. Engineering audits, historical diagnostics, and complete technical boundaries belong in the appendix or this document. The older [finite-feasibility theory draft](../../latex/cross_ebdm/sections/finite_feasibility_theory.tex) remains background; numerous diagnostic boundaries are not separate paper contributions. Written proofs do not mean the current response model satisfies event-sufficiency, coverage, and error assumptions.

1. **Execution mapping and support.** Explain how transport changes command support that pure reweighting cannot create, then pushes it through the target decoder/executor into response space. Capacity-matched task-only has the same expressivity class; support arguments do not establish an inherent expressive advantage over task-only. Candidate gains arise from inductive bias under finite data and optimization.
2. **Task-relevant calibration.** State local excitation and coverage conditions for identifying task responses. Identifying every physical parameter is unnecessary, but directions and timescales governing task events must be covered. System identification is standard; ICDT connects identification information, response transfer, and target tasks.
3. **From responses to task events.** Define event-sufficient response state, source-success margins, calibration/transport errors, model support, and body-failure events, then derive a conditional task-failure bound. Do not assume “better responses improve tasks” and present that assumption as the central proof.

The draft's conditional conclusion in `eq:event-transfer-bound` is:

\[
P(\text{target failure}) \le P(a(Y_s)\le0) + P(0<a(Y_s)\le r)
+ \frac{L}{r}(\epsilon_{\rm cal}+\epsilon_{\rm anchor}) + p_{\rm bad}, \qquad r>0.
\]

Couple true/model source and target responses under fixed matched conditions over a finite horizon. Let `A` denote model support, task compatibility, and satisfied body constraints, with `P(A^c) ≤ p_bad`. Task margin `a` is `L`-Lipschitz under the shared response metric and sufficiently defines success on `A`. Calibration and anchor errors are indicator-weighted expectations on `A`. The proof uses the triangle inequality, Markov's inequality, and a union bound over failure events; it does not assume the desired conclusion that better responses directly improve tasks.

**The remaining issue is connecting theory to the actual model.** Current increment/force descriptors omit recovery guards, cumulative contact constraints, and closed-loop history; current anchoring loss is therefore not a certificate for the proposition. Construct event-sufficient responses, measure or control calibration errors on the visitation distribution, out-of-support probability, and body failures, and address multistep accumulation. Short-horizon prediction MAE cannot directly populate episode-wide error terms. Select contact phase, latency history, and peak/integrated force according to actual success contracts. The bound establishes conditional event preservation; it neither compares task-only nor guarantees sample complexity or full-system task superiority. Retain these limits in the paper and empirically test assumptions and actual increments.

Other boundaries must be corrected together: soft task-band is not a physical-task noninferiority guarantee; uncalibrated ensemble disagreement is not an error upper bound; a zero training gate does not force the shared network's output to equal task-only. Public models, selectors, decoders, and WBC can all determine behavior, so attribution must cover the execution chain. Current guided-contact transport mainly changes tangent and orientation, while the public controller supplies normal holding and some stiffness; this does not show transport learned full compliant control. Theory should describe an implementable finite-budget learner rather than an unattainable population oracle dominating task-only at every state.

## 5. Implementation Order and Architecture Corrections

### Work Order and Deliverable Criteria

| Order | Concrete deliverable | Completion criterion |
|---|---|---|
| **Architecture and naming closure** | Source/configuration snapshots corresponding to frozen evidence; semantic task IDs; thin paper entry points; configured devices/environments; compatible legacy reads | Runtime no longer depends on numbered task identities; representative configurations and actor behavior remain equivalent; historical SHA evidence remains traceable |
| **Contribution and protocol freeze** | Three contributions/RQs, eight core subtasks, four metrics, baseline permissions/full budgets, separate target-mechanism strata and real source qualification | Every experiment answers a defined question; success/constraint criteria, statistical units, and primary aggregation precede new testing |
| **Insertion-mechanism diagnosis and baseline quality** | Locate early over-force; event/dynamic responses; matched actors under one executor; document baseline training and nominal competence | Distinguish implementation differences, public-control boundaries, and algorithmic mechanisms; do not base superiority on poor adaptations |
| **Small RQ development experiments** | Three main ablations, budget curves, mode/event analysis; prioritize discriminative core subtasks | Establish clear mechanism evidence or interpretable applicability limits before deciding what deserves formal freezing |
| **Cross-body comparison and formal main table** | Actual UR5e target; mobile-support extension; all main-table baselines; independent new cohort | Freeze requests, methods, budgets, output paths, and metrics; execute fully and retain real failures |
| **Paper and demonstrations** | Introduction, method, concise theory, general table, three RQ figure groups, representative videos | Every main-text result claim has evidence; identify actual bodies, conditions, and success/failure without selective clips concealing limits |

The next research deliverable is semantic naming and entry-point correction, eight frozen core-subtask contracts, then focused static-insertion over-force diagnosis comparing the full method, no-response, and nonselective constraints under one executor. These results determine whether the compliant fixture can become a primary showcase. Do not immediately enlarge and rerun the old table without checking mechanisms.

### Current Architecture Issues and Migration

Read-only inspection on 2026-11-04 found **49 filenames** containing `t1`–`t4` in `scripts/paper/cross_ebdm`; `qualify_method_behavior.py` has **5,601 lines**, and `train_t4_completion.py` **2,018 lines**. [main_table_execution.py](../../genedynamics/tasks/transfer/main_table_execution.py) reverse-imports `scripts.paper.cross_ebdm` and hard-codes `/tmp` environments, devices, and run-specific paths. Dependency direction and responsibilities require correction, not just filenames.

| Current structure/symbol | Planned migration |
|---|---|
| Runtime task identities `T1`/`T2`/`T3`/`T4` | `obstacle_transfer`, `guided_contact_alignment`, `keyed_insertion_recovery`, `mobile_keyed_insertion_recovery`; retain explicit mappings when reading old results |
| Large numbered-task training/qualification scripts | Algorithms in their existing solvers; task semantics in task adapters; paper scripts only parse configuration and call public entry points |
| Repeated task-number-specific training entry points for ASID, etc. | One method entry point, with differences expressed through existing task adapters/configuration |
| Internal fields such as `T2_TRANSPORT_COORDINATES` | Guided-contact semantic names; versioned compatibility readers for old checkpoints, without modifying binaries in place |
| Runtime imports of paper scripts | General admission/assembly implementations in existing runtime modules; paper scripts call packages in one direction |
| Hard-coded devices, environments, and batch directories | Existing experiment recipes, environment configuration, and launch arguments; algorithm sources do not schedule resources |

Follow MGA's existing [suite entry-point pattern](../../scripts/paper/mga/run_peg_insert.sh): physical-task configurations define named `suites`, retaining `_base.yaml` and `main`/`baseline`/`ablation`/`training`/`validation`/`protocol` categories. Subtasks belong in their task-family configuration, without new top-level `solvers`, `integrations`, `cross_embodiment`, or algorithm-mixed high-level learning trees. Each algorithm owns its solver implementation; common learning retains high-level responsibilities. Check original architecture rules before changes instead of mechanically moving everything into new directories.

**Preserve source/configuration snapshots matching frozen evidence before migrating the implementation.** The 1,703-entry manifest is a provenance index, not a complete content snapshot. Check whether existing training/evaluation snapshots suffice for recovery and preserve exact missing versions only. If a current file changed, recover its original frozen content rather than incorrectly stamping a later file as the original. Do not rewrite v2 results, configuration evidence, checkpoints, or validity records in place. Compatibility readers interpret old IDs; new runs use semantic names and new cohorts. Historical T1–T4 wording may remain for provenance; repository-wide replacement must not corrupt evidence.

Scale formal data to scientific needs. At the existing instance/request density, two subtasks per family imply about **23,200 formal evaluation outcomes**, excluding ablations, budget curves, and new bodies. Complete bounded development and method qualification first, then determine formal sample sizes. This estimate does not authorize automatic launch. Subsequent computation remains GPU0-only; manuscript/document edits do not start background jobs.

## 6. Execution Plan, 2026-11-05: Building Eight Core Subtasks

This is a future implementation plan, not evidence that environments, refactors, or training have run. The November 5 read-only review confirmed v2 remains `FORMAL_TABLE_ASSEMBLED`, with all 36 cells and 11,600 outcomes; the 49 numbered paper files, reverse imports, and hard-coded resources also remain. Retain four families, two core subtasks each, all nine main-table methods, and three main ablations. First obtain development evidence answering the questions, then freeze the complete table.

### Update the Submission Schedule First

The [official RSS 2027 call](https://roboticsconference.org/information/cfp/) and [dates](https://roboticsconference.org/) checked November 5 state: Stage 1 deadline **2026-12-04 23:59 AoE**, maximum **5 text pages + 1 reference page**. Preliminary or incomplete experiments are allowed, but methods and hypotheses must be complete. Stage 2 full papers are due **2027-04-16**, must implement the review-defined Extension Charter, and allow 8 text pages. Retain the current 8-page ICDT draft as the full version and prepare a shortened Stage 1 version in the same LaTeX project. Passing layout checks does not establish Stage 1 length compliance.

Use two delivery horizons: before December 4, deliver a clear method, three testable RQs, credible preliminary comparisons, and one clean mechanism experiment. Continue the full eight subtasks, budget curves, UR5e, and possible hardware evidence without forcing everything into Stage 1. Follow official extension requirements when available; an invitation is not guaranteed.

### Shared Structure and Construction Acceptance

Use the [MGA entry point](../../scripts/paper/mga/run_peg_insert.sh), [suites in _base.yaml](../../configs/arm/peg_insert/_base.yaml), and unified experiments runner. These are proposed semantic suites, not eight new algorithm directories:

| Family | Core subtask 1 | Core subtask 2 | Optional third |
|---|---|---|---|
| Constrained held-object transport | `dual_route_carry` | `aperture_brake` | `sequential_aperture_reorientation` |
| Guided-contact alignment | `dual_channel_contact` | `corner_guided_seating` | `edge_slide_pivot` |
| Connector assembly recovery | `jam_recovery` | `compliant_fixture_insertion` | `insert_then_lock` |
| Body-support-coupled assembly | `stance_insertion` | `reposition_then_dock` | `disturbed_docking_recovery` |

Physical geometry/dynamics belong in existing `envs/domains/manipulation/transfer_workcell`; task identity, observations, public interfaces, and event criteria in existing `tasks/transfer`; algorithm training/inference in each solver; paper entry points expand configuration, call implementations, and summarize. Keep the existing separate static-insertion and compliant-fixture configuration directories, grouping them into one paper family. High-level learning maintains genuinely shared data/checkpoint capabilities, not algorithm training.

Keep `configs/<embodiment>/<task>/{_base.yaml,main,baseline,ablation,training,validation,protocol}`. Results follow the existing `results/<embodiment>/<task>/<category>/<method>/level_<suite>/seed_<seed>` framework. Distinguish formal batches through existing recipe/protocol mechanisms and declared output roots; never overwrite old evidence or introduce project/run_id layers. Suites express subtasks/conditions; devices, seeds, and total budgets are centrally configured. Methods cannot silently enlarge budgets within a suite.

A “built” subtask requires physical scenes/resets, public observation/action permissions, independent event/success/violation criteria, qualified source skills/public feedback, train/development/holdout distributions, nine method adapters, raw physical evidence, and one successful/typical-failure visualization. Criteria read physics rather than self-reported policy phases. CPU-check ordinary execution, wrong ordering, endpoint shortcuts, over-force, and support failures before small development runs. Guided contact and force peaks require physics-substep evidence.

### Constrained Held-Object Transport: From Path Semantics to Pose/Velocity Coordination

**`dual_route_carry`: inherit the dual-route task.** Retain the rigidly attached L-shaped object, upper/lateral obstacle routes, terminal-pose holding, and collision/joint limits; no regrasping is included. Add complete source-trajectory reachability and new-interface equivalence checks. Freeze the source prior across target bodies for the same task. Existing tolerances are 5 mm/3°, 0.3 s holding, and 10 s timeout; any new-protocol changes require separate records. Latency/payload remain within-subtask mechanism tests, not additional subtasks.

**`aperture_brake`: add a physical opening and short docking region.** Add a framed aperture and post-exit terminal region to the [existing workcell](../../genedynamics/envs/domains/manipulation/transfer_workcell/workcell.py). Source skill: approach→orient object→complete passage→decelerate→hold target. Define clearance using the whole object envelope, not TCP passage alone. The evaluator checks complete object passage, direction/order, no frame collisions, terminal pose, and low-speed holding. Flying around the frame, crossing only with the TCP, or arriving after collision cannot count as success. Known aperture geometry is equally public; true latency/payload remain hidden.

Start with generous clearance/braking distance to establish feasibility, then narrow geometric/timing margins and add predefined mechanism shifts. Choose geometry from joint feasibility and source skills, not after observing ICDT rankings. RQ1 distinguishes visually similar trajectories from timely actual braking. At success ceilings, also inspect completion time and violations; do not assume transport must win.

The optional third task adds differently oriented apertures and intermediate turning room, requiring a new multiphase source skill and two ordered passage events. Do not start it before completing the eight core tasks.

### Guided-Contact Alignment: From Load Holding to Corner Contact Switching

**`dual_channel_contact`: retain the strict contract.** Reuse physical dual-channel guides, contact materials, and the [1 ms substep evaluator](../../genedynamics/tasks/transfer/guide_evaluation.py). Loaded contact must begin before mode exit, cover at least 50% of the specified interval, and have no detachment longer than 150 ms. The declared post-exit lift-and-transfer remains allowed. Do not redefine it as uninterrupted contact or rewrite v2.

**`corner_guided_seating`: add a horizontal L-shaped guide.** Begin with a floor, two sidewalls, rounded transition, and physical end stop, avoiding curved-surface normal changes and mesh-seam confounds. Source skill: establish contact→slide segment 1→turn under load→slide segment 2→settle at stop. Public state adds geometry-derived local tangent, path progress, and next-segment target. Contact-geom identities may serve the independent evaluator but cannot silently become privileged policy inputs.

Change geometry, task specification, source skill, and evaluator together. Existing [ContactConfig](../../genedynamics/tasks/transfer/contact.py) constrains scenes/profiles, so YAML alone is insufficient. Current contact normal is world +z, with positive `command[12]` requesting downward preload. Horizontal corners can reuse this convention; spatial surfaces would require explicit local-wrench coordinate mappings.

Completion requires ordered traversal of both segments and the corner, declared loaded contact throughout the guidance interval, stable arrival at the stop, and no over-force. Freeze new contact coverage, allowed detachment, and load ranges separately. Intermediate events prevent lifting over walls, skipping the corner, or flying directly to the endpoint. Increase difficulty from shallow turns to right angles/smaller radii, then cross friction and latency. RQ2 asks when to retain contact structure versus adjust timing/direction. Public-feedback permissions, projections, and selectors must be equal across methods to identify transport's effect.

Optional `edge_slide_pivot` adds a physical pivot and final rotation, requiring new torque/contact-mode criteria rather than merely a different terminal pose.

### Connector Assembly Recovery: From Static Hybrid Events to Compliant Environments

**`jam_recovery`: prioritize the existing static task.** Geometry already includes a keyway and antirotation shoulder. [Recovery criteria](../../genedynamics/tasks/transfer/contact_reference.py) require withdrawal after real loaded contact: at least 12 mm upward after first exceeding 0.3 N, with modes determined from actual trajectories. Retain loading→unloading/withdrawal→realignment→insertion→loaded holding. Direct insertion without recovery is not a substitute for this task. All current 185/320 ICDT failures are over-force, so inspect effective actions, projections, contact phases, and substep force peaks around first impact to distinguish execution, prediction horizon, and source-constraint timing.

**`compliant_fixture_insertion`: complete closed-loop control on the passive fixture.** The [physical implementation](../../genedynamics/envs/domains/manipulation/transfer_workcell/contact_workcell.py) has a world-x passive slide, spring/damper, and mechanical travel, without fixture actuators. Development settings are 0.5 kg, 800 N/m, 12 Ns/m, and ±12 mm travel—starting parameters, not frozen final difficulty. The [task wrapper](../../genedynamics/tasks/transfer/compliant_socket.py) provides measured fixture pose/twist and relative-coordinate evaluation while hiding mass/stiffness/damping.

Still needed: dynamic-task source skills, nine training/deployment adapters, substep fixture displacement/velocity records, and stability contracts. Current control-endpoint maxima are diagnostics, not full fixture-stability criteria. Evaluate relative depth, orientation, recovery, and loaded holding in the current fixture frame, adding terminal fixture-speed/stability-window and travel constraints. Moving the whole socket to a world target or pinning it against a mechanical stop must not bypass the task.

First compare fixed/stiffer fixtures and verify measurable induced motion, then add softer/lower-damping conditions and held-out stiffness–damping combinations. Expand response modeling beyond average window errors to contact establishment, unloading, force peaks, fixture motion, and reinsertion. Closed-loop comparisons determine whether the task supports the contribution. This is RQ2's leading showcase candidate; existing micrometer-scale short-horizon prediction gains are not manipulation gains.

Optional `insert_then_lock` requires physically rotatable bayonet/L-slot geometry and a locking event. Adding rotation commands to the current antirotation keyway does not create a locking experiment.

### Body-Support-Coupled Assembly: From Stance Execution to Goal-Directed Coordination

**`stance_insertion`: retain quadrupedal stance manipulation.** Reuse Go2+Z1 floating dynamics, foot contact, stance WBC, and body-height/tilt/support/joint/QP-failure criteria. Correct the description “body completely stationary”: the [current configuration](../../configs/quadruped/mobile_keyed_insertion_recovery/_base.yaml) already requests `[0.005,0.005,0] m/s` body motion for 6 s. Stance means fixed foot support, not a motionless base; gait/navigation is absent.

**`reposition_then_dock`: add goal-directed repositioning and stabilization events.** Reuse robot/connector assets and add docking sites checked for joint stance/arm reachability. Skill: observe site→adjust body within current foot-support region→satisfy low-speed/posture-stability window→start docking recovery→insert and hold. This differs from fixed-duration body drift; renaming or changing drift speed is not a new subtask.

The shared interface must permit bounded body targets/requests and equal observations/control permissions. WBC executes requests while maintaining support, without secretly solving high-level repositioning for one policy. New action channels require all affected actors/adapters to be updated, not only ICDT. The evaluator verifies ordering from actual base/tool/fixture/foot-contact records. Slipping or moving feet cannot bypass the support region. Design goal-related repositioning within jointly feasible regions rather than inducing failures through mostly unreachable resets.

Increase difficulty from small body shifts to stronger arm–body coordination, then support-gain shifts or insertion reaction forces. This primarily tests RQ3 morphology feasibility, with lower priority than static/compliant insertion and no obligation to establish universal baseline superiority. Only the optional third task adds reproducible disturbances and recovery deadlines. Actual walking is separate; the eight-task plan does not imply navigation development.

### Advance Methods, Baselines, Ablations, and Theory Together

First, record and match the complete execution interface η, distinguishing requests, projected actions, recovery overrides, and online selectors. Second, calibrate responses at critical events using required contact phase, causal latency history, relative fixture motion, and physical force peaks. For guided-contact warm/residual saturation, inspect effective action differences first; if necessary, adjust output scale/permissions for both full method and reference, never ICDT alone.

All nine methods continue across all eight subtasks. ASID/AdaptSim are already strong task-adapted references; preserve their identification/adaptation loops and full budgets. DIAL remains a task adaptation using the public response model. Give FADA one bounded source closed-loop diagnosis: source teacher→collection distribution→action mapping/IDM→learned rollout, then decide whether additional source training is needed. Current learned source/nominal results are 0/64 for all three fixed-arm tasks; `admission passed` indicates identity/execution auditing only. Retain actual failures in the adaptation row, but do not use these zeros as the principal evidence of beating the authors' full system. Update stale SOURCE/fidelity status; ACP and Human2Sim2Robot remain outside the table.

Keep three main ablations: no target calibration; remove response but retain task-band; retain response but remove gate. Match data, warm start, action permissions, recovery, selector, and training/selection budgets within comparisons. Main-table task-only remains but does not replace the pure response ablation. MLP/iterative parameterizations remain appendix diagnostics.

Theory stays centered on the two lemmas and conditional event bound: specify each subtask's event-sufficient responses, margins, and support. Independently test whether predictions correspond to events and distinguish model errors from public-executor changes. Ensemble spread is an empirical filter, not an error certificate. Mathematics states when events can be preserved; prespecified experiments establish strong-baseline superiority and finite-data benefits. Some subtasks may tie or regress; require repeatable main gains and consistent mechanisms, not victory in every cell.

### Comparison Scale, Statistics, and Videos

Retain four main metrics: constrained success, physical violations, failure-penalized normalized completion time, and end-to-end p95 latency. Weight subtasks equally within families; aggregate four families in the text and show all eight in supplementary tables. Worst mechanism groups, mode recovery, peak force/contact curves belong in RQ plots; preserve existing failure boundaries. Emphasize RQ1 budget–success/prediction curves; RQ2 response retention/deviation, event timing, and three ablations; RQ3 body changes, latency, and feasibility.

The basic table retains Panda→xArm7 and Go2+Z1. Prespecify one UR5e subtask per fixed-arm family, preferably aperture braking, corner guidance, and compliant fixtures, crossed against the original body under identical task conditions. All nine included methods must run, not ICDT alone. UR assets/IK availability does not establish qualification/results. Each family needs at least one source/target video pair under matched conditions, naming robot, mechanism, and success, with all key events shown. Label mobile comparisons Go2+Z1, not UR.

Development uses a few independent actors and paired shared-reset requests; formal instance counts follow development variance and minimum meaningful effects. Separate training, development, and formal rosters. Methods on one body share physical resets; across bodies, match task geometry/reset semantics rather than full-state hashes of different robots. Formal statistics retain actor/reset-block levels, paired modes, and mechanism/support strata. Do not fabricate eight training seeds for fixed references. Prespecify primary comparisons; other intervals are descriptive.

0/30/60/120 s remain candidate budgets to freeze, including target exploration, calibration, selection, and failed attempts, with active methods free to allocate interaction. Publish source-data/simulated-training costs separately. One current static-insertion instance uses 10 target episodes/1,616 control steps for AdaptSim and 3/137 for ASID, not an equal-budget efficiency comparison. Time deployment observations/models/selectors/projections/WBC together; actor-forward time is not end-to-end latency.

At current density, the table contains `9×8=72` method–subtask cells and 23,200 evaluation episodes; full coverage of three ablations adds 9,600, totaling **32,800**, excluding training, qualification, budget curves, and UR. Nine-method evaluation on three UR representative tasks adds about 6,960. These are capacity estimates, not frozen sample sizes or launched jobs.

v2 lacks reliable overall start/end timestamps. This round extrapolated from episode `wall_seconds` in the first actor report for each of 36 method–family cells, weighted by instance counts: about 34.84 serial hours for the old four tasks, or 69.68 hours for eight equally complex subtasks. This covers episodes only, excluding training, JIT, startup, CPU audits, and queueing. Fixing early failures lengthens episodes, and new scenes alter throughput. Measure a few rollouts per method/new subtask before estimating GPU0-only scheduling; do not promise an unsupported overnight finish.

### Implementation Order and Near-Term Targets

These are work targets, not guarantees of research effects or timing. Update dependencies/evidence when new issues arise instead of repeatedly using the formal set for development.

| Work window | Required work | Deliverable and successor condition |
|---|---|---|
| November 5–10 | Preserve frozen sources; design/implement semantic identities and thin entry points; eight subtask contracts; substep recording and full-chain timing | New configurations expand, representative old actors remain behaviorally equivalent, provenance is recoverable; do not rewrite v2 |
| November 10–20 | Parallel static-insertion over-force/matched-ablation work and corner-guide/compliant-fixture closed-loop construction; bounded FADA source diagnosis | One complete attributable contact comparison; executable new-task source/public control and shortcut-resistant evaluation |

| After Stage 1 until next freeze | Build all eight subtasks; adapt nine methods; goal-directed body coordination; UR representative tasks; full three-ablation coverage | Freeze formal protocols, budgets, rosters; GPU0 execution, CPU audits, complete main table/RQ plots |

Hardware work requires confirmed arms, force/pose sensing, and fixtures. Prioritize a guided-contact or compliant-insertion task directly tied to the contribution. Continue simulation while resources remain unconfirmed, without presenting Sim-to-Real as validated. Optional third subtasks, complex surfaces, locking, and dynamic walking come later without reducing the eight-core-task goal.

First implement **source preservation + semantic task entry points + eight subtask contracts; then parallel static-insertion mechanism corrections and two contact environments.** Wait for stable interfaces, baseline identities, and evaluation contracts before starting the new formal table.
