## Current Progress: GPU1 Attribution Comparisons, Dynamic Responses, and Baseline Nominal Diagnostics

T2's `configs/arm/guided_contact_alignment/protocol/backbone_attribution_v1.json` froze MLP/four-step deterministic DDIM-style transport × task-only/selective before new training. Within each backbone, task-only/selective start from the same warm checkpoint. Each instance shares branch samples, response model, source bank, output channels, residual radius, and public executor across four cells. Eight instances completed 96,000 updates on physical GPU1 in 959.5 seconds, without new physics calls or opening confirmation data. All 32 checkpoints passed two-mode construction, finite-output, and latency preflight: about 291k/308k parameters for MLP/iterative models, median forward latency 0.30/0.72 ms. Eight subsequently frozen fresh requests—two shifts × two modes × two reset slots—ran once paired across eight instances/four cells, totaling 256 development episodes, not the formal four-task table. Diffusion directly backpropagates the original ICDT objective without score matching, so it is only an iterative transport parameterization, not a learned probabilistic score or additional sampling uncertainty.

While physical evaluation ran, CPU action-support diagnosis used only 1,600 frozen public validation states, uniformly sampling 256 and verifying every eight-instance/four-cell checkpoint SHA. Median per-instance fractions of five modifiable T2 normalized residual channels near the common 0.00625 radius were 96.7%/97.1% for MLP task-only/selective and 99.2%/99.0% for iterative models. Median per-instance mean absolute request differences across all 13 channels between selective/matched task-only were only 1.03e-5 (MLP) and 4.71e-5 (iterative), although some per-instance maxima reached the full 0.0125 diameter. Definitions/evidence are in `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/backbone_attribution_saturation.json`. On the same states, eight warm checkpoints already had 92.9%--98.5% (MLP) and 92.8%--100% (iterative) five-channel boundary occupancy; hashes/statistics are in `backbone_attribution_warm_saturation.json` in that directory. Saturation preceded branch separation. This is expressive-support diagnosis on visited validation data, not independent task evidence. Public projection and a limited residual radius may suppress source anchoring's identifiable request effect; adjust both branches under matched execution permissions rather than only selective loss weights.

Final four-cell physical development results: MLP task-only 63/64, MLP selective 62/64, iterative task-only 64/64, iterative selective 64/64, all with zero constraint violations. Mean failure-penalized normalized times are 0.94873, 0.95013, 0.94839, and 0.94627. Stratified paired resampling of eight training instances and two shared request blocks per mechanism×mode gives MLP selective−task-only success difference −1.56 pp, descriptive 95% interval [−9.375,+6.25] pp. Both iterative cells are 100%, giving within-batch zero difference/interval, not equivalence across all mechanisms. Iterative selective−task-only time difference is −0.002125, interval [−0.00664,+0.00266], without stable time benefit. The original runner separately resampled requests 0--3 and 4--7, preserving mechanism but not mode proportions. Preserve its `report.json`; corrected paired intervals in `results/arm/guided_contact_alignment/validation/backbone_attribution_development_v1/independent_cpu_audit.json` supersede the old values. Subsequent code was fixed and regression-tested.

Independent CPU re-audit verified all 256/256 instance×method×request combinations, result files, and evidence SHAs. Discrete success/failure, modes, constraints, and evaluator provenance agree. Bitwise recomputation matches 186/256 records; the other 70 differ only in floating scalars by at most 8.67e-19, below the explicitly recorded 1e-12 absolute tolerance. The audit separately records strict counts and tolerance-based conclusions. Preserve all 3 timeouts without reruns. Median episode-level p50 for complete actor action calls is approximately 1.04 ms for both MLP cells and 1.48 ms for both iterative cells, demonstrating extra online cost. Near-ceiling T2 success, warm saturation, and highly overlapping actual requests mean this batch establishes neither a source-response task gain nor robust iterative superiority over MLP from 64/64.

The dynamic T3 single-axis fixture segmented-response network completed 600 updates on physical GPU1. Inputs contain only causal public robot/fixture history and issued requests; outputs are 3/6/10-control-tick responses and control-frequency force peaks. Initial 910 training/914 validation windows came from four trajectories. In 127 fixture-motion event windows, 3/6-tick position MAE is 13.30/18.45 μm, worse than zero-displacement 8.48/16.50 μm; 10-tick MAE is 18.57 μm versus zero-reference 26.78 μm. All horizons slightly outperform zero on stronger >100 μm events. This is a development predictor for observed small motions, not reliable extrapolation to unknown contact mechanisms or a substep safety certificate. Earlier automatic denial of a particular concurrent job was superseded by explicit user authorization to share GPU1; training obeys this round's GPU1 resource limits.

With identical data, network, and 600-update budget, v3 additionally optimizes deployed “event probability × conditional displacement.” An independent script recomputed 5,480 previously visited validation windows. `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/fixture_event_objective_comparison_20261031/report.json` records overall 3/6/10-tick x-displacement MAE v2→v3 of 7.65→4.33, 9.17→8.71, 13.15→12.30 μm; grouping by true segment events gives 24.38→23.95, 41.24→35.83, 48.89→42.79 μm. Stationary 6-tick error rises 3.29→3.75 μm; conditional-amplitude error without event-probability multiplication also worsens at 6/10 ticks. Improvements therefore concern current deployed outputs and visited data only. The analysis verifies v3's four source snapshots, recipe, data, and checkpoint SHAs. Neither version adds physics calls. This development prediction diagnosis establishes no task gain, unknown-mechanism generalization, or substep safety.

To avoid reporting only visited validation data, freeze both checkpoints and collect 8 fresh CPU public trajectories with task-local `fixture_response_holdout_*` configurations: four each at 50 N/m soft and 800 N/m stiffer fixtures, all with new reset seeds. All 8/8 raw records pass auditing, without episode-seed or trajectory-SHA overlap with either training bundle. The recipe retains 3,623 windows; fit/validation format labels only package data, and all eight trajectories are external holdout for both models. Frozen report `results/arm/compliant_keyed_insertion_recovery/validation/fixture_response_model_holdout_20261031/report.json`, SHA `68b281ff688cb45b07f8f92867ed83e1fad545e70dcea5ef5b011954375cf3b7`, verifies raw evidence, public trajectories, models, and evaluation-source SHAs. Overall 3/6/10-tick x MAE v2→v3 is 7.72→4.47, 9.16→9.02, 13.79→13.81 μm; event windows 24.30→23.64, 40.26→37.46, 50.63→49.38 μm; nearly stationary windows 5.40→1.78, 3.38→3.73, 5.55→5.85 μm. Both fixture types improve at 3 ticks; stationary 6/10-tick errors increase, and overall errors on the stiffer fixture slightly increase. v3 improves short-horizon prediction on fresh resets while longer-horizon tradeoffs remain. These eight prediction-holdout episodes are not task-success evidence, model updates, or substep safety proofs.

ACP T1's fixed single-demonstration 512-update GPU1 diagnostic is complete: identical-start rollouts at 0/128/256/512 all timeout. Mean teacher-forced translation error worsens 3.93→5.93 mm and terminal error 82.9→102.5 mm; nominal learner competence is unestablished. H2S2R T2 paired diagnostics use identical initial models, 131,072 transitions per branch, and four shared monitor points, all without nominal success. The old termination branch ends in timeout; the new reference-end branch ends over-force, with tracking error 76.9 mm versus 60.3 mm. This establishes neither learning gains from lifecycle repair nor failure of native human-video H2S2R. FADA additionally verifies exact AST equality for 22 upstream training-pass methods and source SHAs in `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/baseline_pilots/fada_native_pass_identity.json`. For each T1--T3 adapted checkpoint, all 42/42 planner tensors remain bitwise unchanged and 82/82 IDM tensors update, recorded in `fada_target_planner_freeze_identity.json` there. These establish partial fidelity; scripted teachers, DAgger collection, nominal competence, and independent instances still require separate checks.

To distinguish ACP's native 100 ms sparse-waypoint representation from learning error, perfect future labels reconstruct interpolated references for the same 60-control chunk at visited states from eight native19 teacher trajectories. Mean reference-position error is 0.160 mm, maximum instantaneous error 3.081 mm; per-trajectory hashes/definitions are in `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/baseline_pilots/acp_sparse_oracle_offline.json`. Sparse temporal representation usually approximates positional references on those recorded teacher paths. This is not closed-loop perfect-label replay and does not exclude distribution shift, orientation/stiffness errors, or phase-switching failure.

The authors' `baselines/ACP/README.md` names public datasets `flip_up_230.zip` and `vase_wiping_200.zip`. This adaptation gives T1 only six fit/two validation native-action demonstrations, substantially fewer, and replaces visual inputs with public low-dimensional history. Current 0/8 target pilot performance establishes failure of this limited-demonstration adaptation, not original ACP under rich data. To call it a strong external main-table method, first declare/provide sufficiently numerous, task-diverse adaptation demonstrations, preserve its distinct supervision permissions/costs, then evaluate two independent instances on disjoint nominal/shifted pilots.

The CPU read-only audit `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/baseline_pilots/acp_teacher_diversity_audit.json` checks actual training manifests/SHAs and eight trajectories per task: T1--T3 use the later qualified `scalar_2175` set; T4 uses its existing qualified set. Each task has three fit/one validation trajectory per mode, all eight qualified and no manifest-marked canonical duplicates. Initial `demonstrations/teacher_collection` had mode failures and must not be mistaken for current training data. All eight demonstrations within each task share one goal; maximum mean paired same-mode TCP distances at matched control indices are only 1.83, 1.11, 5.88, and 0.059 mm. This is not full-state coverage measurement, but changing seeds alone cannot establish coverage of new goals, contacts, or mechanisms. Full GPU1 ACP training should wait for genuine diversity across modes, splits, goals, and mechanisms in a new collection recipe, retaining failures and supervision costs. Eight positive teacher controls are not learned ACP nominal competence.

A later T1 development experiment prespecifies three individual mechanism changes and one held-out combination in `configs/arm/obstacle_transfer/validation/acp_native_impedance_teacher_mechanism_diversity.json`; collection uses no physical GPU. Eight teacher demonstrations across two modes all succeed, pass exact replay/evidence audits, and contain no canonical duplicates. Median paired same-mode TCP path distance from original nominal demonstrations is 4.67/4.11 mm (modes 0/1), but goal geometry stays fixed. The merged 16 demonstrations contain 12 fit/4 validation trajectories and 3,260/1,104 sparse native19 windows, with no trajectory duplicates or split overlap. Qualification is in `results/arm/obstacle_transfer/baseline/acp/demonstrations/mechanism_diversity_sparse_dt100ms/report.json`; full provenance/path statistics extend the diversity audit above.

One 8,000-update ACP development training using `configs/arm/obstacle_transfer/training/acp_mechanism_diversity_pilot.json` completed on physical GPU1 in 337.47 s, retaining all four fixed checkpoints. The lowest held-out denoising loss is 0.01235 at 6,000 updates. Mean native-reference translation reconstruction error on 256 validation windows is about 7.88 mm, not closed-loop task error. CPU rollouts from the original fit mode-0 and held-out mode-1 demonstration resets both timeout, ending 28.28/117.11 mm away. Both evidence audits pass; see `results/arm/obstacle_transfer/baseline/acp/diagnostics/mechanism_diversity_nominal_learning_cpu/report.json`. These extra mechanism demonstrations do not establish nominal learner competence; lower training loss cannot admit ACP to the main table.

To test whether 100 ms sparse waypoints and 12-waypoint execution alone cause failure, four CPU closed-loop oracle comparisons use **perfect future labels** on the same two visited teacher trajectories. Per-control labels and sparse chunks each succeed 2/2, with all four audits passing, recorded in `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/baseline_pilots/acp_sparse_oracle_closed_loop/report.json`. This only excludes inevitable sparse-execution failure under perfect labels on those two trajectories. Future teacher labels are unavailable online, so oracle results are neither baseline nor generalization performance. Diagnose first learned-path divergence, sampling noise, and feedback conditions before deciding to expand supervision to more goal geometries.

The same report recomputes initial divergence from original per-control evidence: both learned trajectories issue different xyz requests at control 0 and exceed 1 mm TCP-path divergence by control 5. Corresponding perfect-label sparse-oracle maximum deviations over recorded teacher prefixes are only 4.80/2.38 mm. Failure begins with learned requests, not only after several 1.2 s replans, but this does not separate causal contributions of training error, sampling variance, and input conditioning. Before admission, compare repeated samples at identical resets and teacher-forced first chunks with a fixed checkpoint; do not merely add demonstrations and relabel old experiments formal.

The same report adds fixed-checkpoint first-chunk multisampling: keep each original reset's public initial state fixed and generate the first native19 request for eight predefined sampling seeds, without stepping. Decoded xyz-velocity-request L2 differences from the teacher's first request, before public bounds, are 0.132--0.643 m/s (median 0.204) for mode 0 and 0.098--0.726 m/s (median 0.254) for mode 1: two resets, 16 network calls, zero task steps. The adapter converts predicted absolute reference pose minus previous public reference into velocity by dividing by 20 ms. Millimeter position errors may therefore become large initial velocity requests, then be clipped by public bounds. This is a **candidate mechanism inference** from code and first requests, not isolation of training error versus controller semantics. Before repair, do not call this the original ACP method's contact-transfer limit.

A read-only ACP provenance audit verifies 20/20 manifest-file hashes across the main repository and external force encoder. Of 90 comparable same-name method ASTs, 82 match exactly; eight differences are listed in `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/baseline_pilots/acp_source_identity.json`. Twelve CPU tests for numerical alignment with ACP's source pass, as do 21 FADA/H2S2R source/training-contract tests. These strengthen port fidelity without establishing learned nominal closed-loop competence for ACP, FADA, or H2S2R.

Task-side four-cell development uses public episode/evidence workflows and records complete actor action-call latency. After training, preflight all 32 checkpoints on actual public validation states for both-mode construction, finite output, and GPU1 p50/p95/p99 latency. Only then freeze eight new requests and execute paired development. The independent four-task table still awaits external learned-baseline mechanism, nominal-competence, and pilot qualification closure.

To diagnose initial response-model coverage, CPU collection retains five additional groups of four complete public trajectories: soft fixture, extremely soft fixture, ordinary contact-x pulse, pulse beyond nominal lateral clearance, and extremely soft fixture plus pulse. All 24 trajectories across six groups pass re-audit without success filtering. The last four groups were designed sequentially from earlier diagnostics and are development data, not preregistered independent tests. Lowering stiffness to 50 N/m alone or applying bounded lateral pulses does not substantially exceed approximately 0.23--0.35 mm maximum fixture motion; logs show nearly constant TCP/fixture relative x during pulses. The frozen mixed bundle has 5,471 fit/5,480 validation windows, with 352/389 >100 μm events. Same-architecture/seed/600-update GPU1 comparisons run sequentially, retaining both models.

Mixed-model GPU1 training subsequently completes. Aggregating >10 μm event windows by episode against zero displacement, 10-tick prediction improves on 10/12 validation trajectories, 6-tick on 6/12, and 3-tick on 0/12. Six-group matched-window comparisons are in `results/arm/compliant_keyed_insertion_recovery/training/segmented_response_mixed_gpu1/coverage_contrast.json`. Three-member ensemble spread has near-zero or negative Spearman correlation with absolute errors across horizons; see `uncertainty_diagnostic.json` there. Spread is therefore not a calibrated error upper bound. Later excitations among the 24 trajectories were designed after inspecting earlier results; these guide the next excitation/model version, not independent mechanism-generalization estimates. Theory now distinguishes horizon/contact-mode errors, event margins, and uncalibrated ensemble spread.

Recompute the four complete `validation/contact_x_probe_engaged` logs over the 15 prespecified positive-pulse controls: mean issued world-x requests are 4.75--5.31 mm/s, but net TCP-relative-to-fixture x is only about −0.8--4.0 μm, while fixture x displacement is 0.192--0.256 mm. Near-synchronous motion, inferred from public observations, indicates insufficient relative contact excitation. Increasing the same pulse amplitude does not replace a newly and independently declared dynamic-mechanism design. This is read-only analysis of existing logs, with no new physics calls or validation samples.

## Current Implementation: Method Definition Alignment and Dynamic-Task/Nominal-Competence Diagnosis

The user authorized item 1 after brainstorm review and parallel item 2. Scope: precise method definitions, finite-budget theory, minimal passive-compliant T3, and existing-baseline nominal-learning diagnosis. A diffusion backbone, formal table, and new external baselines remain later work and are not automatically admitted by these changes.

### Method Identity and Theory Alignment

Actual YAML parsing, SHA-bound checkpoint loading, and CPU actor construction verify 16 cells—ours, task-only, ungated, band-only across four tasks—and 144 instance/mode actors. Evidence: `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/method_identity/report.json`. No resets/physics occur; construction success is neither behavioral equivalence nor performance qualification. In particular, current T2 YAML references `actor_bindings_mode_conditioned_contact_establishment_v2.json`; inspecting old neighboring `actor_bindings.json` alone misidentifies the deployed method.

| Task | Actual full-method identity | Attribution implication |
|---|---|---|
| T1 | `ours_gated_v2`; `PublicPlannedObstacleDenseActor` | Same actor class as task-only; training/selection budgets still need comparison |
| T2 | `ours_mode0_contact_established_transport_v5`; shared terminal wrapper includes contact-establishment admission | Transport modifies only five tangent/orientation channels; normalized residual radius 0.00625; public task controller owns normal direction, stiffness, and target force |
| T3 | `ours_task_first_mode_specific_dual_physical_selected_v21`; `PublicModeSpecificRecordedCompletionDenseTransportActor` | Mode-specific refinement, physical checkpoint selection, online response admission; old ungated/band-only are not one-loss-term-matched versions |
| T4 | `ours_gated_v2_completion_projected`; `PublicCompletionDominantRecoveryExecution` | Task-only still uses a liveness wrapper; total main-table differences cannot be attributed to anchoring; same-start projection factors are analyzed separately |

The correct current candidate form is `Bound(base_proto + residual)`: source chunk, source/target history, and controller memory are conditioning inputs; a public task controller supplies the residual center. Public decoder, bounds, and execution binding then determine requests. Default H=10 with 20 ms control period gives a 0.2 s candidate horizon. Actors with online selectors must count all candidates/model queries in latency. Training gate, offline checkpoint selection, and online admission are distinct objects.

Initialization provenance follows actual artifacts. Full T1 and task-only come from separate historical training directories; construction auditing does not certify matched warm starts/optimization budgets. T2 `policy_instances_v3/report.json` and `train_t2_policies.py` record six branches copied from one warm checkpoint/optimizer state with shared branch samples. T3 mode-specific identity binds the trained task-only shared-start report, followed by recentering, fresh optimizer, per-mode continuation, and candidate selection. T4 `completion_tail_aligned_transport/checkpoints/report.json` continues an earlier completion-aligned continuation, while main-table task-only is a historical frozen checkpoint. Preserve each report/sample stream; the four tasks are not collectively a same-warm/same-budget single-factor experiment.

Paper method/theory now distinguish the population oracle from finite-data/optimization-budget task-only learners. The response bridge is an additional condition, not an automatic sample-complexity benefit of the loss. Two-checkpoint bounds require the same distribution, short horizon, proxy, execution interface, and sampling protocol; they exclude multi-checkpoint search, online switching, and episode success. Training `G=0` removes that sample's response term; it is neither online fallback to task-only nor a guarantee of zero conditional effect.

Future four-cell backbone comparisons must rebuild matched MLP/new-backbone experiments together. A common warm start may come from pretrained task-only, but both subsequent branches must share checkpoint and continuation budget. Full objective differs from task-only in both band and response; band-only and same-band ungated controls must isolate each. Preserve old results under original identities.

### Dynamic T3 and Data Boundaries

The new task is limited to `compliant_keyed_insertion_recovery`: single-axis passive spring-damper socket, independent task-local configuration/contract, unchanged original static-T3 defaults. Public world observations add fixture pose/twist; robot joint arrays exclude fixture DOFs. Public geometry control uses measured fixture-relative coordinates, converting current relative velocity requests to world execution while historical requests retain world semantics. Fixture mass, stiffness, damping, and simulator internals are not policy inputs.

Public trajectories explicitly use `public-target-task-data-v3`/`public-fixture-world-v1`; old v1/v2 reject new fields. Response data uses `public-fixture-segmented-response-v1`: four past world-frame robot/fixture observations, issued world requests, 3/6/10-tick TCP pose/twist/wrench and fixture position/twist increments, with control-frequency force peaks stored separately. Future fixture state is labels only. Execution, evaluator, trajectory ingestion, and response windows share single-axis validation, rejecting lateral displacement/velocity and nonidentity rotation; a valid rotation matrix cannot bypass `passive_x_v1`. Old response checkpoints do not silently accept the new schema, which does not imply a trained dynamic model. Split complete episodes into fit/validation and retain failures.

Initial CPU qualification is complete in `results/arm/compliant_keyed_insertion_recovery/validation/cpu_qualification/report.json`. The prespecified 150-control contact-free recoil check shows spring-damper energy decay. Four complete public-geometry trajectories—two xArm7 modes × fit/validation—achieve 4/4 success, zero violations, zero backend/evaluator state mismatch, and independent evidence-audit passes. Contact induces about 0.229–0.272 mm maximum fixture motion. This establishes executable task/data infrastructure under mild development conditions, not learned-transport superiority or performance across three source-applicability classes.

The audit also fixes robot/fixture DOF separation in generic IK: optimize/clip robot joints only, preserve initial fixture coordinates, and recognize UR multistart from the actual six robot DOFs. All four prespecified requests rerun in `validation/cpu_qualification_robot_ik/report.json`, retaining 4/4 success, zero violations/mismatch, and identical control counts/actions. One public-log digest is identical; three have observation floating differences up to approximately 4.55e-13. `recheck_comparison.json` preserves the comparison without claiming identical old/new SHAs.

The repaired logs convert via `scripts/paper/cross_ebdm/prepare_fixture_response.py --config configs/arm/compliant_keyed_insertion_recovery/training/segmented_response_data.json` into `results/arm/compliant_keyed_insertion_recovery/training/segmented_response_data/robot_ik`: 910 fit/914 validation windows, 943D features, 10×13 requests, 3×27 segmented responses. Windows never cross episodes; public-log digests and bundle roundtrips are checked. No new physics, success filtering, or model training occurs. Keep original qualification/data bundles. Contact motion is below 0.8 mm clearance and 2 mm success tolerance; freeze discriminative compliant conditions next, rather than claiming dynamic adaptation from four successes.

Compatibility fixes also constrain five existing static-T3 training/calibration entry points to v1/v2/static environments after loading; two completion-array entry points reject fixture fields, preventing silent old-model training after discarding fixture observations. Related 22 tests pass; dynamic and old public-data/response tests add 20 passes; dynamic task/contact/evidence tests give 53 passes. After moving qualification execution into the task layer, 29 of those pass again—do not double-count. The unified runner previously generated one successful qualification trajectory before the IK fix; preserve that provenance without claiming execution under current source. New paper commands remain thin; architecture documentation records remaining thick legacy completion trainers.

### Baseline Nominal-Learning Diagnosis

Nominal competence requires closed-loop execution of learned policies. ACP's 32/32 is a teacher positive control; six fit demonstrations per task and bounded reconstruction do not replace learner competence. H2S2R's four-task 1M-transition training is complete but nominal monitors never succeed, establishing neither convergence nor absence of data/objective issues. FADA retains valid source positive controls while distinguishing complete native acquisition from task-adapted source acquisition.

This round uses a small prespecified CPU nominal-request set at ACP's original fit/held-out demonstration starts and H2S2R selected/final checkpoints, interpreted with existing learning curves. Do not replace new failures. Demonstrable port-semantic fixes use independent recipes, preserve old defaults/results, and receive bounded learning comparisons. Target mismatch need not be repaired until success; without established nominal learning, failures are not evidence of beating original SOTA. `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/baseline_pilots/nominal_learning_diagnosis_closeout.json` binds all original reports/SHAs.

The first 16 CPU nominal episodes all fail. ACP tests one original fit and one held-out demonstration start per task: both T1 requests timeout; both T2/T3/T4 requests fail over-force. The issue therefore extends beyond held-out generalization. H2S2R compares selected/final checkpoints at one shared fresh nominal seed per task: T1 timeout/collision; T2 and T3 timeout/timeout; T4 timeout/joint_limit. Reports are `results/<embodiment>/<task>/baseline/<method>/diagnostics/nominal_learning_cpu/report.json`. These small samples diagnose learning chains, not paper performance.

The confirmed H2S2R port difference concerns reference lifecycle: native force tracking terminates at reference end and disables early success termination; the adaptation holds the last frame until shared task termination. Native tracking already has no success bonus, so its absence is not a bug. This round adds a default-off reference-end training-termination option, leaving shared success/failure contracts unchanged: partial lifecycle alignment only. Prefix/reward comparisons and a bounded native PPO smoke are separately accounted, not proof that learning is repaired. ACP uses fixed single-demonstration/update-budget fitting diagnostics, reporting denoising loss, sampled reconstruction, and actual rollouts separately rather than equating loss reduction with nominal task learning.

| ACP T1 single original-fit trajectory continuation | Fixed batch8, LR5e-5, 128 updates; same-start rollouts at 0/32/64/128 all timeout. Fixed-sampling teacher-forced mean translation errors about 2.86/4.21/4.34/5.01 mm; terminal errors 64.8/97.1/84.0/76.4 mm; 127 s | Failure already occurs at a training start. Check execution timescale/state distribution and closed-loop shift from local prediction; this budget proves neither insufficient capacity nor method failure |
| H2S2R T2 reference-termination comparison | Same-checkpoint actions/rewards match exactly for first 503 steps; old environment continues to 1000 with about 254.77 extra reward; new setting marks done at reference end without success or task timeout | Confirms lifecycle inequivalence; retain explicit opt-in fix, without calling it a performance gain |
| H2S2R T2 paired same-initialization continuation | Per branch: 8 actors×16 horizon×64 epochs=8192 transitions; full-task monitors at epochs 0/16/32/64. Parameters/normalization match tensorwise at 0/16, diverge at 32. Both branches timeout at first three monitors and lose axial support at the last; 0/8 success; 264.7 s | Final tracking error falls to 28.5/24.6 mm while episodes fail support at about 55% of reference. This is not task gain; inspect task-constraint/native-tracking-objective compatibility |

This round adds 30 baseline nominal-diagnostic episodes, 128 ACP updates, and 16,384 H2S2R training transitions, plus a 128-transition native-learner interface smoke. No new shifted-target tests or GPU use. Task-local GPU0 recipes for larger ACP single-demo and H2S2R paired continuations are written but unlaunched: respective caps 512 updates/1800 s and 131072 transitions per branch/3600 s total, each limited to 4 GiB. CPU failure does not automatically authorize four-task retraining.

Final review moves physical GPU UUIDs from algorithm source into recipes; runtime still checks physical device, `cuda:0`, and `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0`. Both recipes pass plan-only checks. ### Order After This Delivery

1. Preserve frozen results/full actor identities. Freeze comparable internal ablations' initialization, execution wrappers, online selection, and training/validation budgets first. External system-table methods may retain native control structures.
2. With the dynamic-T3 interface, establish independent compliant conditions and whole-episode splits, then train/validate segmented response models, especially contact/recoil errors. The four current geometry trajectories only qualify infrastructure.
3. Diagnose ACP/H2S2R nominal learning in parallel, freezing budgets/hypotheses before expansion. Keep FADA's valid source and complete missing fidelity evidence. Run prepared bounded recipes only when GPU0 is free.
4. Implement few-step diffusion through actual `base_proto + residual`; check near-zero initialization of the full sampling chain, input gradients, effective requests, and the 20 ms deadline before MLP/diffusion × task-only/selective comparisons. Explain diffusion and anchoring gains separately.
5. Freeze the independent table only after four-cell and external-baseline pilots qualify. T1 remains simple-mechanism diagnosis, dynamic T3 contact adaptation, and T4 morphology extension. Choose pivot/wiping extensions to fill specific missing mechanism evidence, not all candidates at once.

## Paper Contributions and Next Experiments (2026-10-30)

### Scientific Goal and Evaluation Scope

The primary comparison is full-method task performance, data needs, and execution cost against mature external methods; task-only is a capacity-matched internal ablation. Transport need not outperform task-only on every task, instance, request, or metric. Preserve/explain regressions, but do not reject the entire paper for any adverse pair. T1--T3 measure manipulation performance; T4 establishes transfer of the same skill interface to a mobile body without requiring transport to improve success further.

System comparisons and component attribution require different evidence: compare complete systems after reasonable method-specific training, but match other factors when attributing gains to response anchoring, gating, or calibration. Task-level admission driven by target success rewards changes data permissions and the algorithm; do not add it automatically after old gates fail. Prioritize baselines/visualization and pause extra ours wrappers/selectors intended solely to satisfy old strict gates.

The revised story has three levels. First, system results: full Cross-EBDM should offer consistent, interpretable overall gains over mature learned baselines on T1--T3, while allowing other methods to lead individual tasks/costs. Evaluate prefrozen macro-average, failure penalties, violations, and data/compute costs instead of every best cell. T1 checks retained ceiling capability; T2/T3 carry contact/recovery differences. Second, mechanism: task-only measures conditional transport increments. Easy in-distribution cases may tie; majority or practically meaningful improvements under response mismatch/contact/recovery suffice, without universal per-task/request dominance. Third, scope: auditable xArm7, UR5e, and Go2+Z1 successes establish semantic-interface execution across DOF/support morphologies. T4 provides morphology feasibility, not primary performance superiority.

The next version stops requiring transport to beat task-only on every task/metric. RQ1 centers on equally weighted T1--T3 macro constrained success, with failure-penalized time, worst group, violations, and resources. Support comes from leading a prefrozen primary aggregate or a better performance–resource Pareto point, plus improvements over strong baselines on most tasks or prespecified difficult subsets. Show regressions/costs; one adverse cell does not negate the contribution. RQ2 uses task-only/mechanism ablations, permitting no gains on saturated T1. Reproducible, meaningful gains on anticipated difficult T2/T3 conditions without clear systematic harm support conditional transport rather than pointwise dominance. RQ3 requires auditable successes with the same semantic interface on xArm7, UR5e, and Go2+Z1; T4 need not lead performance.

Three formal admission rules prevent a return to every-cell victory criteria. First, RQ1 uses equally weighted T1--T3 macro performance: best or statistically indistinguishable from best, with meaningful gains on at least two tasks or a prefrozen difficult T2/T3 subset, supports the system contribution even if a baseline leads a task/time/resource metric. Second, RQ2 requires stable positive increments over task-only on T2/T3 aggregate or difficult subsets, with T1 preserved and no worse safety costs—not benefits at every seed/request/task. Third, RQ3 needs frozen-protocol successes, constraint audits, and reproducible videos; T4 stays outside the primary T1--T3 ranking. If only some conditions hold, narrow claims instead of post hoc test/metric changes to obtain universal wins.

### Current Results and Supported Narrative

The complete four-task table is `results/_protocol/four_task_transfer/confirmation_v14/statistics_contract_complete/main_table.json`. It records online-evaluator outcomes and passes input-integrity checks. The formal ledger lacks complete physical trajectories, so every physical criterion cannot be independently recomputed offline. This limitation does not automatically make executed runs invalid or unexecuted.

| Method/identity | T1 | T2 | T3 | T4 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Ours full system | 320/320 | 320/320 | 320/320 | 638/640 |
| DIAL-MPC task-adapted | 320/320 | 0/320 | 284/320 | 640/640 |
| Ordinary response MPC | 320/320 | 313/320 | 308/320 | 488/640 |
| Public geometry/feedback | 40/40 | 38/40 | 40/40 | 80/80 |
| Old FADA v14 task adaptation (invalid final comparison) | 0/320 | 1/320 | 0/320 | 0/640 |
| ACP v14 placeholder rejection (target not executed) | 0/320 | 0/320 | 0/320 | 0/640 |
| Human2Sim2Robot v14 placeholder rejection (target not executed) | 0/320 | 0/320 | 0/320 | 0/640 |
| Task-only (ablation) | 320/320 | 317/320 | 320/320 | 447/640 |

Ours records no physical-constraint violations across four tasks. Many T1 methods reach ceiling, making it a basic-capability/cost reference. T2 tests contact robustness; T3 recovery, completion efficiency, and difficult mechanisms; T4 already has morphology-demonstration value. Strong public geometry means high absolute success alone does not establish a unique learned-method contribution.

Aggregating only T1--T3 under the next paper scope, ours exceeds ordinary response MPC by 1.98 pp macro success, lowers failure-penalized normalized time by 0.0291, and improves worst-group success by 6.25 pp. T1 success ties; T2 gains 2.19 pp and avoids one violation; T3 gains 3.75 pp and reduces normalized time by 0.0990. Against DIAL-MPC, T1 ties, T2 gains 100 pp, and T3 11.25 pp. Against public geometry, T1/T3 tie and T2 gains 5 pp while removing that reference's 5% violation rate. The latter has one deterministic identity/40 requests per task, so comparisons are descriptive. This supports the candidate story “retain simple-task ability, benefit contact/recovery tasks,” but not SOTA superiority: v14 FADA is under-qualified, and ACP/H2S2R are unexecuted placeholders whose zeros are not performance.

New baseline foundations provide real development evidence, but none meets paper admission. FADA source-preserving T1 curves at 0/30/120 s are all 0/8 timeout; T2/T3 curves also 0/8; T4 identity-matched pilot 0/8 over-force. Executions have no infrastructure errors; source controls/evidence audits pass. ACP completes 32/32 native-impedance teacher controls across four tasks, four diffusion-policy trainings, and held-out native-action numerical checks. Latest sparse native target pilots: T1 0/8, T2 1/8, T3 0/8, T4 0/8, chiefly timeout, over-force, and support loss, with no execution/audit errors. Robot-source H2S2R completes 1M transitions each on T1/T2/T3: T1 pilot 0/8 timeout, no violations, 8/8 mode match; T2 0/8 timeout, no violations, 0/8 mode match; T3 0/8 with 2 over-force/6 timeout and 0/8 mode match. T4 completes 1M transitions; selected epoch128's independent target pilot is 0/8 (7 over-force, 1 timeout), no execution errors. Without the authors' human-video assets this is not native human-to-sim-to-robot reproduction. These establish executable adaptations and closed-loop failures; fidelity review, independent instances, and frozen formal requests remain necessary.

Task-only addresses component increments. v14 T1--T3 macro success difference is +0.3125 pp and normalized-time difference −0.00185: T1 ties success/slightly slower; T2 adds 3 successes/slightly faster; T3 ties/slightly faster. A separate difficult T3 set yields 64/64 versus 62/64 and 2.30% faster mean time on joint successes. These suggest no systematic T1--T3 degradation and conditional T2/T3 benefit, but final actors and old six-cell ablations are not fully aligned, so this is not final single-factor response/gate causality. T4's 638/640 versus 447/640 is a large system difference, used next only for morphology/supplementary ablations, not to dominate RQ1's manipulation conclusion.

More detailed evidence:

- Independent difficult T3 comparison: `results/arm/keyed_insertion_recovery/validation/finite_time_mode_specific_dual_physical_selected_transport_confirmation/gpu1/report.json`, 8 instances/64 paired requests, full method 64/64 versus task-only 62/64, no violations. Across 62 joint successes, mean reduction 13.34 controls (2.30%); 7/8 instances have nonnegative time direction. McNemar p=0.5 does not establish significant success improvement. Request-level time sign tests ignore shared training instances; add actor/request-block intervals and failure-penalized time. This measures a complete mode-specific-refinement, physical-checkpoint-selection, runtime-response-admission pipeline, not a response-weight-only experiment. Its actor entered v14, so the two reports are not fully independent confirmations.
- v14 T3 six-cell table: gated-full 320/320, ungated-full 284/320. Bindings compare `ours_task_first_mode_specific_dual_physical_selected_v21` with old `ungated_full`; +11.25 pp is a full-pipeline difference, not isolated gating. Realign before answering the gate RQ.
- T4 same-start 2×2: `results/quadruped/mobile_keyed_insertion_recovery/validation/transport_completion_factorial/same_start_response_anchoring_gpu1/report.json`; task-only/anchoring × projection off/on all achieve 64/64. Both work under these support conditions, without extra anchoring success gain. This null result does not prevent morphology extension.
- v14 `transport_claim.json` reports `core_claim_supported=false`. The old automatic gate equally weights T1--T4 and rejects stable transport for two adverse T4 pairs versus task-only; ACP/H2S2R also remain coverage-only placeholders, leaving strong external comparisons incomplete. This `false` is the old strict contract's result, not rejection of the next paper's contributions. Separate T4 morphology and aggregate only T1--T3 for RQ1. Require overall leadership/statistical co-leadership among credible external learned baselines and gains on most tasks or prespecified difficult subsets. Final judgment still awaits credible FADA/ACP/H2S2R target evaluation.

### Three Research Questions and Candidate Contributions

| Question | Contribution addressed | Main tasks/comparisons | Missing work |
|---|---|---|---|
| RQ1: What task, target-data, and compute advantages does the full method offer over mature methods? | Complete intervention-calibrated executable-distribution-transfer method | T1--T3; FADA, ACP, H2S2R, DIAL/MPC, public feedback; show all four tasks | Credible baseline training/evaluation/resource table; cannot yet claim superiority over ACP/H2S2R |
| RQ2: When do calibration, response matching, and selective constraints help, and at what cost? | Conditional source-response regularization and task/behavior tradeoff | Mainly T2/T3; controlled task-only, no-calibration, no-gate, shuffled-correspondence comparisons | Align final actors, training/selection budgets; calibration curves, conditional events/modes, hierarchical uncertainty |
| RQ3: Can the same task semantics/skill interface transfer across kinematics and support morphologies? | Cross-embodiment executability and scope | Panda→xArm7; new Panda→UR5e; Panda(T3 skill)→Go2+Z1 | Actual UR trials; existing-body GIFs/videos/reproducible trajectories; T4 need not beat task-only |

Current supported content includes high-completion system capability, conditional full-method gains on difficult T3, mobile-morphology extension in T4, and limits of source anchoring. Low-budget advantage, independent response-term task gains, superiority over ACP/H2S2R, and hardware sim-to-real require evidence. Theory retains conditional links among feasible support, response/model error, and event margin. Public-executor attribution identities are explanatory tools, not separate original theoretical contributions. Finite-training generalization/regularization gains require measurement; properties of exact task-optimal solutions neither establish nor refute them.

The strongest candidate contribution is therefore not universal task-only dominance, but calibrated selectively admitted transport preserving easy-task capability and improving contact/recovery success or efficiency when source semantics remain valid and target execution responses undergo identifiable shifts. Existing T1--T3 data support this direction, but matched task-only causality and three external learned baselines remain incomplete: write a candidate narrative, not final superiority.

### Theory Supporting the Revised Contribution Narrative

Retain the proposition that an exact task-only optimum for true task cost cannot automatically be dominated by adding response regularization; it rules out universal task advantage inferred from the objective alone. But this oracle is not the experimental comparator. Actual task-only is estimated under finite target data, updates, model capacity, and frozen selection budgets. Separate population optimum, finite-budget task-only, and selective-transport learners so an unattainable oracle does not negate possible finite-data inductive-bias gains.

Refocus theory on three testable questions. First, calibrated source responses under declared task/mode conditions may provide useful finite-budget inductive bias. A positive bridge only yields conditional cost comparisons when error is below margin, not lower estimation error or sample complexity by itself. Test target-data needs using T2/T3 budgets, held-out response errors, and constrained success. Second, training selective gates control response gradients: `G=0` is neither execution fallback nor zero effect. Online selectors require actual execution-selection events and the same augmented state for attribution. Compare full/task-only/ungated/shuffled correspondence by mode/event, including false admissions and coverage. Third, executable support/public decoder/WBC define deployment scope and constraints, not learned-transport task superiority. T4/UR5e videos validate scope; controlled T1--T3 comparisons establish performance attribution.

Prioritize a finite-budget oracle inequality or sample-complexity comparison under a local strong-convexity approximation, separating task-only estimation error, transferable response signal, false-admission quality, calibration error, and source bias into observable/bounded terms. If data cannot estimate them, retain a qualitative decomposition instead of unverifiable global Lipschitz, safety, or task-dominance theorems. If gains occur only on difficult T2/T3 conditions, theory must use that same condition set. T1 ties and no T4 increment are expected boundaries, not counterexamples.

### Actual External-Baseline Gaps and Execution Order

All three already have native PyTorch cores in `genedynamics/solvers/single/<method>`, task-controller interfaces, and development pilots; engineering integration is not starting from zero, but paper-level integration is unfinished. v14 ACP/H2S2R are placeholder failures and FADA is under-qualified, none valid SOTA-table evidence. Each route needs six admissions: fidelity, source/known-nominal positive controls, reasonable training opportunity, frozen checkpoint selection, independent target requests, and resource accounting. Once these pass, preserve genuine target failures instead of repairing until success.

1. **FADA: freeze a faithful final adaptation.** Planner/IDM numerical checks, source controls, and T1 source-preserving 0/30/120 s curves are complete. Check upstream oracle/DAgger, frozen planner, and IDM-only target adaptation, then repeat small pilots with at least two independent training instances. If numerical/source/mechanism gates pass but target fails, retain that cross-embodiment result without tuning for target success.
2. **ACP: establish learned nominal rollouts and diagnose data scale.** Four-task native-impedance teachers, diffusion policies, held-out native-action checks, and target pilots are complete. Failures are no longer “no trained policy” or old dense/sparse action-time mixing. Check reference/virtual pose, time-varying stiffness labels, and 100 ms horizon against upstream; test fit/held-out demonstration starts before bounded data-scale comparisons. Fixed-stiffness controls explain adaptive compliance but do not replace external main-table results.
3. **Human2Sim2Robot: finish four tasks and qualify the reproduction name.** T1/T2/T3 each complete 1M transitions/target pilots; T4 training is complete; nominal learning remains undiagnosed across four tasks. Check object-trajectory reward, initialization, action scale, domain randomization, and checkpoint selection, then independent-instance pilots. Name this route `Human2Sim2Robot task-adapted (robot-source trajectory)`; native human-video reproduction awaits author assets.

### Next Main-Table, Ablation, and Statistical Organization

- **8×4 main table:** ours, FADA, ACP, H2S2R task-adapted, DIAL-MPC, response MPC, public geometry, source prior; every external baseline runs all four tasks. Task-only belongs in a separate ablation table, optionally appended to the main table for orientation, never external SOTA. T4 is an extension column, not part of a mandatory universal-win gate with T1--T3.
- **Common metrics:** constrained success↑, actual physical constraint violation↓, normalized completion time capped at timeout for failures↓, worst-group success↑. Separately report rejection/execution counts; rejection is not physical overload. A resource table gives target-log seconds/demonstration counts, training transitions/GPU hours, online p50/p95 latency/model calls/deadline misses. Episode wall time is not inference latency.
- **Resource fairness:** compare actual source/log/demo/model provenance. Ours/DIAL/response MPC currently share response models, primarily differing in online optimization; hard-coded permission labels alone do not establish unfairness. Itemize ACP target demonstrations and H2S2R source trajectories. Permit native resources for full-system performance–cost comparison, and separately compare compatible routes under shared logs. Only the latter supports same-information attribution.
- **Internal ablations:** keep six cells, first aligning final actors and ablation identities. Match warm start, sample stream, training/validation budget, public executor, and checkpoint rule; count physical checkpoint-selection target visits. Focus mechanisms on T2/T3 and retain T1/T4 null increments.
- **Research conclusions:** freeze primary tasks/metrics/comparison families before testing. Default system primary metric is equally weighted T1--T3 macro success, with all task/risk/time tradeoffs shown. Majority-task or explicit-mechanism-subset gains may support limited contributions without requestwise dominance. Report effects and actor×request-block intervals, adjust selected comparisons for multiplicity, and avoid repeated seeds, altered gates, or post-test winning subsets. Report T4 scope/completion separately.

## Historical Record: Confirmation v1 and Subsequent Development

Architecture migration and GPU0-only confirmation are complete; old statements about failed migration, unvisited confirmation, or only five instances are obsolete. Old multi-GPU attempts remain in invalid_runs and are not reincorporated. `confirmation_v1` contains 21,200 scored records, 14,720 physical executions, and 6,480 predeployment rejections: 9×4 main table, 6×4 ablations, eight independent learned/calibrated instances. Deterministic references have one 40/80-request set; rejection slots are not independent training repetitions.

**Execution integrity passing does not mean the paper contract fully passes.** Three distinct issues were found:

1. Original `aggregates.json` groups worst cases by mechanism only; the frozen design requires **mechanism × requested mode × body/support**. Independent derived statistics correct 12 scalar cells; originals/SHAs remain unchanged.
2. All four frozen contracts require observed mode=requested mode, but the executed evaluator never independently compares them. Formal artifacts lack observed mode and stepwise trajectories, preventing offline reassessment. Existing rates are only **recorded evaluator success**, not complete-contract-certified constrained success. Adding mode conditions may reduce counts; do not guess which episodes fail.
3. Formal T4 omitted frozen `public_folded_reset_v1` and `.002` joint noise, using default zero perturbation. Receipt hashes mix sample ID/requested mode; the ten public-reference requests within each mechanism×support×mode group share one action hash/terminal signature. Snapshot and executed-source SHAs agree, confirming the reset contract was not implemented. Missing complete integration states prevent reconstructed original receipts. **T4 intervals are conditional on repeated actual resets, not the declared randomized-reset distribution.**

A saved CPU counterexample uses an existing successful T3 development path with actual mode0: changing requested mode to1 still yields success=True from the frozen evaluator, without physics. Do not delete/overwrite the run or unilaterally change its invalid label; retain historical execution evidence with explicit evaluation limits. Repairs require a new version/independent confirmation cohort, not tuned reuse of these seeds as fresh tests.

## Statistical Definitions and Limits

Frozen v1→v2→v3 designs were recovered from architecture `source-overlay.zip` with per-member SHAs. The primary scientific contrast is gated full−task-only under B-log, using 10,000 hierarchical paired bootstrap samples. Training/calibration instances are top-level; sample reset blocks within fixed mechanism/support strata, jointly sampling both modes for a reset. All sampled instances share request-block resampling, avoiding treating one request package as eight independent packages. Seed267100 follows development rehearsal. Shared request axes and precise Holm-family grouping are transparent implementation clarifications, not claims they were itemized at freeze time.

Intervals are pointwise 95% empirical percentiles conditional on current mechanism/support combinations. Supplementary exact instance sign flips enumerate all 2^8 assignments, conservatively applying Holm within each main-table/mechanism question family over three mean metrics×tasks×contrasts. They assume symmetry of instance differences conditional on evaluated requests, not randomized experimentation. Nonlinear worst-group metrics receive bootstrap intervals only. Methods with different permissions receive descriptive differences/intervals, not superiority tests.

The reset audit further limits T4: ten nominal slots are not ten randomized physical resets. Saved intervals remain reproducible descriptions of records, not generalization-error estimates for the frozen reset distribution. Do not “repair” old runs into formal evidence by dropping duplicates, changing denominators, or inventing random seeds.

All-rejection rows retain full denominators/frozen penalties (success=0, violation=1, capped time=1), without treating eight slots as trained instances or inventing training CIs. Separately report actual-execution violations, blank when nothing executed. All-success/all-failure empirical intervals may have zero width; this neither proves probabilities 0/1 nor establishes equivalence.

The main ours/task-only and ablation gated_full/task-only rows share 3,200 scored records with identical action hashes, scores, and steps, not a second independent evidence set. Execution wall_seconds includes 12-worker contention and is not control-call p95.

## Three Failure Classes: Confirmed Findings and Next Changes

### T2: Strict Contact Conflicts with the Public Route; Change the Binding

The public xArm route still lifts→moves laterally→presses down at the endpoint, while source contact motion and target aerial transfer share a phase label. ## Limits on Gate, Model, and Theory Changes

Distinguish training anchoring gate, model-validation checkpoint selector, online source-distance fallback, and T4 public recovery-prefix gate.

- All16 T3/T4 training instances' final candidates pass selection; checkpoint fallback=0/16.
- Saved minibatch anchoring means are78.15% forT3 and46.69% forT4; task-compatible fractions about99.98%/99.72%. These are not formal-trajectory gate coverage.
- Formal ours network calls equal controls, excluding source-distance fallback in this implementation. T4 computes candidates each step before deciding execution, so network calls do not reveal learned-command activation. Formal common/learned counts were not saved and cannot be reconstructed.
- Current response outputs/task proxies focus on TCP, wrench, and force peaks, excluding joint margins, support state, and complete hybrid events. Checkpoint proxy toleranceδ=.05 therefore implies neither actual safety, mode match, nor task nondegradation.

Decide method changes after public execution is repaired. Response-schema revisions should add phase, guard memory, issued/applied action history, and task events/joint margins; independently validate errors/event margins before retraining. Low success alone does not justify more matching weight or training. Anchoring within the same policy class does not increase expressivity; its contribution requires same-permission task-only evidence in data efficiency, conditional event correspondence, or task gains.

Narrow theory to conditional model selection: response correspondence requires model-error/event-margin conditions. Training gates are not online safety shields; a locally disabled gate loss does not make the final policy task-only. Unknown uniform-error, Lipschitz, and identifiability constants remain assumptions. Without hardware evidence, do not call this simulation completed sim-to-real.

New diagnostics require sharper theoretical scope; do not present unverified propositions as established paper conclusions:

1. **Define complete event success.** Include physical termination, actual mode match, and contact criteria. Small continuous TCP-response error does not guarantee discrete event ordering. Require positive event-boundary margins or explicit event-error terms.
2. **Include control memory.** Delay, issued/applied references, phase, guard latch, and integrators yield different futures at identical TCP/wrench. Current schema is not sufficient Markov state; one-step fit does not certify complete recovery correspondence.
3. **Separate boundedness from completion.** T4 joint-safe timeout demonstrates constraint feasibility is neither task reachability nor finite-time hybrid completion. Add separate reachability/progress assumptions; nominal QP residual does not bound dynamics error under hidden gain.
4. **Execute the stated distribution.** Risk/nondegradation conclusions require physical reset diversity and cross-method pairing. T4 v1 repeated resets do not match the declared distribution; notation changes cannot repair this.
5. **Contributions are increments under common conditions.** Public guards/contact references/WBC repairs must serve all applicable baselines. They are shared capability, not exclusive transport gains. Then use matched task-only/new ablations to establish transport improvements; otherwise retain “not demonstrated.”

### 2026-10-21 v11 CPU Offline Closure, Three Failure Classes, and Risk-Model Decision

The final repaired qualification version is `behavioral_predeployment_20261021_v11`. All 1,440 CPU qualification episodes were independently reevaluated, yielding 1,241 successes, 102 timeouts, 97 constraint violations, 0 guide-contact contract failures, and 0 infrastructure errors; 36 rows passed behavioral qualification and 16 were rejected, alongside 4 ACP algorithm-level rejections and 4 Human2Sim2Robot external-resource rejections. The four-task public-geometry sentinel achieved 16/16, and all static T2 task-owned interface checks passed for learned dense, ordinary response MPC, DIAL, and the public source prior. The shared T2/T3/T4 contracts therefore remain unchanged, and remaining baseline failures are no longer reinterpreted as shared-executor problems. CPU-derived statistics are under `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/predeployment_20261021_v11_v2/`; the first v11 derived directory, which mistakenly retained decision text from before the v10 repair, has explicitly been sealed as invalid. The original v11 qualification results are unchanged.

The treatment of the three failure classes is frozen. The 102 finite-time progress failures continue to receive method-side diagnosis of phase, issued/applied actions, accepted references, and terminal errors; implementation repairs are allowed only for source-fixture, normalization, decoder, objective, or adapter errors. Closed-loop stagnation after interface correctness is established remains a method limitation. The 97 constraint-control failures are diagnosed using the last safe/first violating 1 ms substep, predicted/actual force margins, and reference accounting; 20 come from T2 ours, task-only, and six ablations, ruling out an isolated checkpoint accident. Guide-contact contract failures currently number 0; retain only a regression gate, without changing the route, force limit, deadline, or evaluator.

For learned-family T2 overforce, CPU evidence has rejected three simple approaches. Every fixed execution-radius scale failed across 384 learned episodes, with 10 overforce events remaining even at the smallest radius; four fixed coordinate scopes also failed to eliminate failures while retaining task translation capability; the existing 50 Hz response head detected 0 of 6 actual overforce events in advance, predicting maxima of approximately 4.5--5.9 N when actual peaks were 28.6--33.4 N. The next version therefore stops shrinking the radius, removing translation coordinates, or reusing the old response force head. Instead, it adds an independent public substep-risk ensemble: inputs are public history and ten-step candidate requests; labels are maximum total/lateral forces over every 1 ms physics substep; one-sided calibration operates on episode blocks; the largest safe interpolation is selected from `[1, .5, .25, 0]`. Zero interpolation must be bitwise identical to the qualified public base.

All 588 newly collected development episodes passed audit, but the original requirement of at least 20 high-risk fit windows per instance failed as recorded; these cannot be presented as eight independent risk datasets. Because risk is a shared contact-physics quantity, a pooled split is frozen only for the prototype: fit/calibration/validation contain 276/44/133 high-risk windows, with disjoint physical initial-state slots 0--3/4/5; the index passed independent episode-by-episode reconstruction. These adaptive development data can validate the architecture only and cannot silently become the paper's final checkpoint. Before formal confirmation, an equal-budget source/known-nominal training and calibration scheme must be frozen and disclosed, or the data must explicitly be designated shared offline pretraining with the full data budget reported.

The next causal comparison changes accordingly. The transport increment must compare ours and task-only under identical substep-risk admission; the risk gate itself requires a `no_substep_admission` ablation retaining the same transport checkpoint, without changing the meaning of the existing anchoring-gate ablation. External baselines retain the authors' mechanisms and verified adapters; adding our new risk head cannot leave the original method name intact. GPU1 next performs only risk-model prototype training and held-out calibration. Integration with the existing T2 actor, rerunning CPU qualification for affected rows, and complete zero-physics/package/device audits follow only after episode-block coverage, high-risk recall, safe-sample rejection rate, zero-fallback identity, and public-sentinel gates pass. The complete machine-readable decision is at `results/_protocol/four_task_transfer/diagnostics/cpu_failure_diagnosis_and_risk_revision_20261021.json`.

The theory is simultaneously restricted to a conditional multiscale statement: shared task/executor, candidate transport, and risk admission are separate layers; risk is defined by 1 ms peaks within a 20 ms actor horizon; episode-block conformal calibration provides marginal coverage only when calibration and deployment blocks are exchangeable, and cannot be called a universal force-safety certificate; zero interpolation guarantees only a return to the public base, not completion before the deadline; along reachable trajectories, the safe admissible set must also be assumed to intersect the positive-progress set. The transport performance contribution must ultimately be supported only by increments over task-only and frozen ablations on a new independent roster under the same risk admission and public executor.

### 2026-10-22 CPU closeout of T2 online risk admission and next-version changes

After a new independent cohort, the GPU1 risk-model prototype passed offline qualification in 8/8 cases; this establishes episode-block coverage, high-risk recall, and rejection rates only on the recorded-command distribution. Subsequent CPU online development validation completed 264 new episodes and independently reaudited all 264/264 evidence files. The result failed as recorded: risk-admitted ours achieved 64/64 and task-only 63/64, with one 33.52 N overforce; ours and task-only without risk admission achieved 57/64 and 62/64, respectively. The new public-geometry sentinel achieved 7/8, also with one 31.94 N overforce. This validation opened no confirmation states and cannot serve as paper performance evidence.

The failure was not a missed risk-model detection. The current selector interprets the final zero-interpolation candidate as the public base and unconditionally overrides the model through `safe[..., -1] = True`. All 128 risk-admitted episodes contained states where the model also classified the public base as exceeding the 20 N/8 N soft limits; ours had 26,136 such control ticks and task-only 25,412, yet the selector forced execution at 26,135 and 25,412 ticks, respectively. In the failed task-only episode, the public-base predicted upper bound exceeded the limit from tick 142 onward, but the base continued executing through tick 199; at the violating tick, the predicted upper bound was 173.88 N/26.06 N and the actual peak was 33.52 N/7.54 N. Many identical forced fallbacks still succeeded, also showing substantial over-rejection on the online candidate distribution. Reducing miscoverage or increasing the calibration correction alone cannot solve this: the current offline data come from recorded behavior commands and do not automatically cover interpolation candidates generated by transport in closed loop.

The public-sentinel and task-only failures share one pattern: both occur during mode-0, stage-2 guide motion, with an x command of 0.0075 m/s and a greater-than-30 N impact in a single physics substep after approximately 4 N at the preceding control tick. The public-sentinel failure reopens qualification of the shared mode-0 executor, not the force limit, deadline, mode, reset, or evaluator. The next version first designs and bounds a stage-2 reference-brake/settling action using public state; any shared repair must apply to every method and the public sentinel and cannot count as a Cross-EBDM contribution. Learned-method requalification is allowed only after passing a completely disjoint new sentinel cohort.

Risk admission v2 makes four structural changes. First, remove the forced-safe flag on the final candidate. Second, make a finite emergency reference-brake horizon an explicit candidate evaluated by the same risk model; if the admissible set remains empty, return `risk_set_empty` and count a task failure instead of continuing to execute model-rejected actions. Third, collect training, calibration, and validation data using the frozen deployment candidate generator, covering transport interpolations, the public base, and emergency horizons; qualification only on old behavior-policy commands is insufficient. Fourth, qualification jointly checks episode-block coverage, high-risk recall, safe-window rejection rate, empty-set rate, finite-time progress, and constraint violations on a new closed-loop cohort, preventing apparent safety obtained by stopping everything.

The current actor does not enter the manifest, and the main table does not start. The release sequence is fixed: shared-executor diagnosis and new sentinel qualification; risk-admission-v2 zero-physics identity/empty-set checks; candidate-distribution-matched fit/calibration/held-out qualification; new paired online qualification of ours/task-only with the same risk gate; complete zero-physics, package, device, and behavioral predeployment checks; only then a completely new independent four-task roster. External baselines share only the public executor and cannot retain their original names after receiving our risk head. The risk-gate increment is answered by the new `no_substep_admission` ablation, not by reusing the anchoring-gate ablation. Machine-readable evidence and revision decisions are at `results/_protocol/four_task_transfer/diagnostics/t2_substep_risk_online_failure_revision_20261022.json`.

### 2026-10-22 CPU offline statistical closeout and shared T2 candidate diagnosis

The deterministic entry point `scripts/paper/cross_ebdm/analyze_shared_failure_closeout.py` combines v11 qualification, the 264-episode online risk validation, and subsequent shared T2 executor diagnosis into one machine-readable artifact. Repeated runs produce identical SHAs. The v11 statistics are unchanged: 1,241 successes, 102 timeouts, 97 constraint violations, and 0 guide-contract failures among 1,440 qualification episodes; 36 rows behaviorally qualified and 16 rejected. These numbers are for qualification and diagnosis only, not paper performance comparisons.

The boundaries of the three qualification failure classes also remain unchanged. The 102 finite-time progress failures continue to receive method-specific diagnosis of phase, issued/applied actions, accepted references, and terminal errors; the 97 constraint-control failures continue to receive 1 ms substep diagnosis of predicted/actual force margins, references, and joint/support accounting; v11 guide-contract failures remain 0. The public sentinel in the later 264-episode development validation achieved 7/8 with one 31.94 N overforce, so it reopens only shared T2 mode-0 executor qualification. It cannot retrospectively change the v11 denominator or justify changing the force limit, deadline, mode, reset, or evaluator.

Sequential shared-T2 diagnosis has rejected simple repairs. A speed of 6.5 mm/s achieved 8/8 on previously visited cases but 31/32 with one overforce on 32 new cases; 6.5 mm/s with the original post-outlet lift achieved 31/32, zero violations, and one timeout; 6.75 mm/s with the original lift achieved 30/32, zero violations, and two timeouts. Connecting the existing 8/8/10 mm three-dimensional reference-lead governor to the 6.75 mm/s route yielded 16/16 for mode 1 but timeouts in all 16/16 mode-0 cases. Mode 0 switched around global ticks 750--753, with median stage-1, stage-2, and stage-3 entry times of 858, 879, and 960, leaving only about 40 control ticks for descent and the 15-tick terminal hold. Terminal states remained near clear height without contact. This demonstrates that full three-dimensional lead clipping repairs reference runaway but breaks deadline liveness along the vertical route.

The predeclared planar-lead candidate was also executed. Its first implementation incorrectly inherited the full three-dimensional controller's three-axis 0.02 m/s speed limit and event logic, violating the declaration of “raw lift with x/y clipping only.” All 32 evidence records are retained, but the run is sealed with `INVALID_RUN.json` and excluded from selection, qualification, and performance interpretation. The corrected implementation passed bitwise unit tests: z, rotation, impedance, and force channels are identical to raw lift, and planar actions change only when x/y lead exceeds bounds. The corrected 32 cases still achieved only 30/32 with zero violations, so the mechanism was rejected without opening new seeds. In the two timeouts, clipping reduced terminal planar reference lead from 55--67 mm to 0.7--3.7 mm, but one episode entered stage 1 only at tick 969 and the other stage 2 only at tick 967. The failures arise from serial-route stage budgets and vertical overshoot, not planar reference runaway alone.

The next version therefore stops scanning speeds, lead limits, or deadlines and stops combining existing serial subclasses. The only permitted structural revision is a concurrent, monotone public route after the outlet: once the declared outlet is crossed, planar motion and lifting occur concurrently within the same free-space segment, followed by monotone descent and the original 15-tick terminal hold. Before implementation, freeze route geometry, events, per-axis issued-reference envelopes, conditional integration, and worst-case stage budgets; the analytical budget must reserve every terminal-hold tick. This route applies equally to public sentinels, baselines, ablations, and ours and constitutes shared benchmark capability. Require 32/32 with zero violations on the 32 previously visited cases, then qualify on at least 64 fresh seed-disjoint public-sentinel episodes; failure of either gate rejects the structure.

Risk-admission v2 still follows the shared executor: remove the final candidate's forced-safe override; include a finite emergency reference brake as an explicit candidate under the same risk model; return `risk_set_empty` and count failure for an empty admissible set; generate fit/calibration/held-out data with the frozen deployment candidate generator; jointly qualify block coverage, high-risk recall, safe-window rejection rate, empty-set rate, finite-time progress, and closed-loop violations. External baselines receive the public executor only, not our risk head. The complete closeout is at `results/_protocol/four_task_transfer/diagnostics/cpu_shared_failure_closeout_20261022.json`; the current state is `revision_specified_not_qualified`, so GPU training, predeployment, and a new four-task roster remain closed.

### 2026-10-22 Authoritative CPU closeout v2: three failure classes and freezing common-protocol-v3

This section supersedes the preceding `revision_specified_not_qualified` state. Diagnosis and attribution rules for the three failure classes are frozen:

- **Finite-time progress failures (102 in old qualification).** T2 serial post-slot planar motion and lifting consume the deadline; T3's 10 mm insertion lead lacks progress margin and retreat has a separate latch; T4's fixed-base reachable set under joint-margin constraints does not intersect the terminal set. The diagnostic unit is a paired episode with the same reset, requested mode, and public executor. Record phase occupancy, per-axis accepted public-reference progress, remaining path/deadline, terminal errors, observed mode, and hold count. Reject any shared candidate that times out, violates constraints, mismatches the mode, or depends on method-private signals on a fresh sentinel.
- **Constraint-control failures (97 in old qualification).** T2 control-period endpoints alias physics-substep contact, and the old contact cell permits single-substep impacts; T4's legacy route crosses joint limits, while adding only joint margins turns joint-limit failures into unreachable timeouts. The diagnostic unit is the last safe and first violating physics substep from the same post-reset state; record total/lateral force margins, issued/accepted/applied references, joint margins, support count, and WBC residual. Failures reproduced by the public sentinel in the same cell are attributed to benchmark infrastructure. After public-sentinel and shared-executor gates pass, interface-correct method-specific closed-loop failures remain in that method's denominator.
- **Guide-contact contract regression gate (0 in old qualification).** This is not positive evidence of repaired failures, only a hard regression gate. Each T2 episode continues to use exact physics-substep evidence to check first contact and outlet, pre-outlet axial-support coverage, longest continuous contact loss, mode crossing, and task-owned request equality. Any failure blocks the corresponding method row and public protocol; thresholds cannot be tuned, episodes replaced, or fallback results substituted.

Shared changes have completed qualification; scalar searches stop. T2 `concurrent_monotone_lift_v3` achieved 32/32 on visited diagnostic cases and 64/64 on a fresh seed-disjoint public-sentinel set, with zero constraint failures and all evidence independently reaudited. It uses 6.75 mm/s, concurrent planar motion and lifting after the outlet, followed by monotone descent and terminal hold. T3 freezes a 15 mm insertion-only lead and safe-settled release, with a development-capability gate of 64/64 and zero violations. T4 freezes `joint_margin_public_reference` and planar body intent shared by all methods; its capability gate achieved 16/16 with zero violations, four-foot support, and a minimum measured joint margin of 0.0002256326 rad. All three are public benchmark capabilities and cannot count toward Cross-EBDM transport gains.

The new contracts are T2 `task_contract_v3`, T3 `public_v6`, and T4 `task_contract_v3`. `verify_shared_protocol.py` completed zero-physics audits of 52 main/baseline/ablation configurations; protocol validity and architecture equivalence passed. Shared contracts are immutable from now on. If v6 behavioral predeployment again exposes a public failure, create a new contract version and invalidate this qualification round rather than editing configurations or executors in place.

The next version first executes all-method CPU predeployment v6. Every main, baseline, and ablation row inherits identical T2/T3/T4 contracts, reset/mode requests, and evidence audits. Source-fixture, normalization, decoder, objective, or adapter errors may be repaired; method-specific timeouts or constraint failures after these checks pass remain algorithm outcomes. Missing author assets remain predeclared availability rejections. Rows requiring new learned artifacts are explicitly marked `training_required`, without fabricated behavioral results. Then implement Cross-EBDM's own risk-admission-v2: remove forced safety on the final candidate, explicitly handle empty safe sets, evaluate emergency braking with the same risk model, and use the frozen deployment candidate generator to produce mutually exclusive fit/calibration/held-out episode blocks.
