This modification cannot be added to external baselines under their original names or presented as a shared-executor gain. Only after final behavioral binding, package, and device gates all pass may a new four-task roster be generated, completely disjoint from development, qualification, and old invalid confirmation data.

The theory follows these evidence boundaries: shared execution feasibility, candidate transport, and risk admission are three distinct layers; forward invariance does not imply finite-time completion; the safe admissible action set must have a nonempty intersection with the deadline-positive-progress set; hybrid-release liveness depends on renewed support and bounded controller carry; T4 supports only constrained reachability under a specified public body intent; empirical receipts are not global force-safety or robust-invariance certificates. The 177 failures form three mutually exclusive, exhaustive classes. Selection requires both calibrated force upper bounds below the frozen limits and a contact-sustainability lower bound satisfying the frozen T2 contract.

Old risk-v1 behavior-policy windows are no longer used for this training. The data unit is therefore frozen as five independent counterfactual branches from the same public state; each restores the same task/actor checkpoint, is billed independently, and retains failures. ### 2026-10-22 CPU closeout after formal v7 and next-version decisions

The 1,440 CPU episodes of v13 qualification were independently reaudited: 1,256 successes, 100 timeouts, 81 overforce events, 3 guide-continuity failures, and zero infrastructure errors. The 184 failures are exhaustively partitioned into three classes. The 100 finite-time progress failures come from DIAL, FADA, and direct source prior; the 81 force-safety failures come from FADA, ordinary response MPC, direct source prior, and three learned-ablation tail samples; the 3 guide-continuity failures come from task-only, band-only, and ordinary response MPC. Formal v7 then exposed generalization gaps absent from qualification. Cross-EBDM achieved 320/320 on T1, 79/320 on T2, 291/320 on T3, and 512/640 on T4. Success-rate differences relative to task-only were -0.3 percentage points on T2, +2.5 on T3, and +8.0 on T4; all three 95% intervals cross zero, so there is still no evidence of transport task-performance superiority. T2's 240 timeouts cluster in both latency modes and payload/mode-0; T4 failures cluster in the latency/derated-support combination.

New same-reset CPU pairings separate attribution for T2 and T4. On T2, Cross-EBDM and public geometry show identical failure patterns across four mechanism-by-mode conditions: both latency modes time out, payload/mode-0 times out, and payload/mode-1 succeeds. T2 therefore has a shared public-route finite-time reachability gap requiring a new task-contract version; repair gains cannot count as a Cross-EBDM contribution. On T4, public geometry achieves 8/8 while Cross-EBDM achieves 5/8; all three failures occur under latency. The shared body intent and joint-margin executor can complete the task, but the learned candidate breaks liveness after the recovery gate, so only the Cross-EBDM method-side execution wrapper may change.

Three bounded T2 probes have stopped scalar searches. Raising the fixed tangential-speed floor to 15--20 mm/s repairs only payload/mode-0; latency/mode-0 still times out and latency/mode-1 overforces. Reducing transit load to 0.75 or 1.0 N still fails to repair latency/mode-0, and one 1.0 N case reaches 30.31 N. Multiplying tangential stiffness requests by 1.25, 1.5, or 2.0 produces bitwise-identical issued/applied sequences because log stiffness in the shared 13D interface has already risen by 0.08/tick to the frozen upper limit of 1.0. Velocity commands are indeed applied in the failed trajectories, but once the accepted reference reaches its fixed lead envelope, physical progress remains below the deadline requirement.

The next T2 version implements only one structural change: public deadline-aware reference admission in the existing `genedynamics/tasks/transfer/controllers/guide_contact.py`. The controller constructs candidate reference increments from remaining path, time reserved for the post-outlet route and terminal hold, measured progress, reference lead, wrench, and contact history. It admits only increments satisfying both force/contact feasibility and positive deadline progress. If the intersection is empty, explicitly return a `progress_set_empty` development failure; neither the 20-second deadline nor force/contact criteria may be relaxed. Bind the new behavior through a new task-local protocol version shared by all methods and public sentinels; old `task_contract_v3` and formal v7 remain unchanged.

The next T4 version adds Cross-EBDM-specific candidate/common completion admission to the existing `genedynamics/tasks/transfer/controllers/recovery_execution.py`. The wrapper already computes common and candidate actions each tick. Outside the terminal set, the new version selects the largest candidate blend preserving progress in the common direction and public joint/support margins; near the terminal set, it must also preserve terminal-set entry and continuous hold accumulation. An empty set falls back bitwise to common. Both actions, blend, terminal state, decision reason, and counts become part of checkpoint state and controller diagnostics. This addition follows a latency timeout whose terminal position/orientation were within tolerance but hold count was only 7; a position-directional derivative alone cannot guarantee completion. Shared resets, body intent, joint-margin executor, recovery gate, force limits, support criteria, and deadline remain unchanged. This gate is only a public-feedback heuristic unless subsequent model-error bounds justify a safety or liveness certificate.

CPU release order is fixed: first add unit tests for state restoration, bitwise fallback, permission boundaries, and failure exits of both controllers; then use fresh seeds for the T2 public-sentinel and T4 candidate/common paired gates; after T2's public gate passes, rerun every executable method; after T4 passes for one instance, extend to eight Cross-EBDM instances; finally rerun zero-physics architecture, package, and device audits. Only after every gate passes may a new formal roster disjoint from v7 and this diagnostic round be generated. GPU1 is used only if new learned artifacts actually require training; both initial modifications can currently be completed on CPU.

The theory is simultaneously separated into three layers: shared execution feasibility, candidate transport, and action admission. Force safety, guide-contact continuity, and finite-time completion are independent constraints; forward invariance does not imply finite-time completion, requiring a separate assumption that safe/contact-feasible actions intersect deadline-positive-progress actions. Passing qualification establishes deployment compatibility only. Transport contributions still require paired increments over task-only and frozen ablations on a fresh seed-disjoint roster with identical shared executors and admission.

They are all marked development-only and `confirmation_states_opened=0` and cannot enter the paper's main table.

### 2026-10-22 CPU rejection gates for controller candidates and next implementation decisions

The two controller candidates proposed above completed CPU development gates, but neither may bind to the formal manifest. Original trajectories, per-control-period diagnostics, independent evidence audits, and SHA lists are archived in task-local results hierarchies; conclusions are in `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/post_confirmation_cpu_diagnosis_20261022/controller_probe_closeout.json`. All runs are development-only, with `confirmation_states_opened=0` and `gpu_calls=0`.

T2 deadline-aware public reference admission ran an initial and a corrected-writeback version, each with 8 episodes. The correction removed only residual reverse-velocity writeback when the lead exceeded its limit; physical conclusions were unchanged: latency/mode-0, latency/mode-1, and payload/mode-0 each achieved 0/2; only payload/mode-1 achieved 2/2, totaling 2/8 with zero constraint violations. At the end of latency/mode-0, required deadline progress was 20 mm/s but measured progress was only 1.793 and 1.741 mm/s, accumulating 536 and 539 `progress_set_empty` control periods. This candidate is therefore rejected, and no `task_contract_v4` is created. Under the frozen 20-second deadline, force limit, guide continuity, and shared 13D action support, no action satisfying both constraints and positive deadline progress was observed in these latency cells; further increasing lead or speed would change the benchmark contract. Future work must explicitly choose between retaining this failure and creating/requalifying a new shared route/action-support contract. Public repairs cannot count as Cross-EBDM contributions.

T4 completion-dominance admission achieved 8/8 on 8 fresh instance-01 episodes, with zero constraint violations and all support gates passing, but nearly collapsed to the public controller. Only 41 of 3,894 candidate-eligible control periods admitted a nonzero candidate blend, an admission rate of 1.05%; 4/8 episodes never admitted a candidate. This candidate also remains outside the actor manifest because it demonstrates only that common fallback restores completion, not that transport yields task performance.

The next version changes only Cross-EBDM's T4 training and selection semantics. Changes belong in the existing gated transport objective under `genedynamics/solvers/single/cross_ebdm/learning`, task parameters under `configs/quadruped/mobile_keyed_insertion_recovery/training`, and entry points remain in `scripts/paper/cross_ebdm`. In addition to existing task-proxy, response-matching, risk, and change terms, add per-step completion progress relative to the public controller: candidate translational and rotational progress toward the target must not fall below the same-state common action, with candidate residual penalized near the terminal set to protect continuous hold. The loss uses only public states, public task targets, candidate/common actions, and the frozen response model, without evaluator outcomes or hidden mechanism labels.

Release order is also frozen. CPU model gates first check bitwise reproduction of the old objective when the new term is disabled, finite gradients, and consistent checkpoint restoration; source/known-nominal validation must jointly satisfy task-only-relative proxy, translational/rotational progress quantiles, and nonzero candidate utility. Only then may eight independent instances train on physical GPU1. New checkpoints first undergo 8 CPU physics development episodes for instance-01: require 8/8 success, zero constraint violations, at least 25% candidate admission among eligible periods, and nonzero admission in every mechanism/support group. Then extend to a fresh eight-instance set, requiring nondegeneration for every instance and retaining per-instance utilization. If these gates fail, retain formal v7 conclusions rather than replacing method results with the common fallback's 8/8.

The theory adds a necessary attribution condition. Safety or contact invariance does not imply deadline completion, and success restored by fallback does not imply effective learned transport. A transport contribution requires a nonempty intersection of safe/contact-feasible and positive-deadline-progress actions, with deployment trajectories actually adopting candidates on a positive-measure set of states and producing incremental completion progress over common. Compatibility of the mean model task proxy does not guarantee hybrid terminal hold, so training, checkpoint selection, and final reporting must explicitly cover translation, rotation, support, terminal-set entry, and candidate utilization.

### 2026-10-22 T4 completion-aligned transport and composite actor release

Initial completion-aligned training completed eight independent instances and 16,000 updates on physical GPU1, but raw checkpoint selection rejected 8/8. Task proxy and candidate utility passed, with utility at 51.0%--61.4%; translational-gap p95 remained $0.80\times10^{-6}$--$1.46\times10^{-6}$ and rotational-gap p95 $7.77\times10^{-6}$--$11.61\times10^{-6}$, above the frozen $10^{-7}$ gate. Training did not crash, and no confirmation states, physics simulation, or target rewards were accessed.

Diagnosis established that training optimized the mean while checkpoint selection checked p95, hiding a few difficult states in the mean. Existing `gated_transport.py` therefore adds separate worst-10% tail terms for linear and angular directions; terminal states are constrained only by the residual term returning to common hold. Without a completion spec, the old objective retains its original path. v2 continued from the eight failed continuations for 12,000 GPU1 updates. Relative to v1, translational p95 fell to $0.34\times10^{-7}$--$1.89\times10^{-7}$ and rotational p95 to $0.83\times10^{-6}$--$10.86\times10^{-6}$, while utility rose to 72.6%--83.6%; task-proxy differences from task-only were only -0.0047 to +0.0046. Raw v2 still failed some $10^{-7}$ gates and was not released as an unwrapped checkpoint.

A composite actor was then frozen: use common hold exactly in the terminal neighborhood; in other recovery states, choose the largest candidate fraction from $\{1,0.5,0.25,0\}$ preserving both common translational and rotational directional progress. CPU zero-physics preflight passed all eight instances on 347 independent validation states: positive candidate admission was 76.9%--92.2%, full-candidate use 72.6%--83.6%, and both maximum post-projection gaps were below $10^{-7}$. This satisfies the offline necessary gate that the composite policy has not collapsed to common, but is not itself evidence of physical success.

The physics development gate first ran instance-01 on 8 new seeds, achieving 8/8 success, zero constraint violations, and candidate intervention in all four mechanism/support groups; 2,755 of 3,591 eligible nonterminal controls used a positive candidate fraction, an admission rate of 76.72%. The eight instances then ran 64 episodes on another paired fresh-seed set, achieving 64/64 success and zero constraint violations. Per-instance admission was 71.81%--80.43%; across 32 instance-by-mechanism/support groups, minimum within-group admission was 576 steps and the minimum proportion 68.36%. Failure replacement was disabled throughout, and zero confirmation states were opened.

After passage, `main/cross_ebdm` in T4's existing `actor_bindings.json` was updated to `ours_gated_v2_completion_projected`, directly binding eight v2 checkpoints and `public_completion_dominance_v1`; no parallel manifest was created. Full catalog validation passed. Both CPU and GPU1 zero-physics package audits passed 52 executable rows and retained 8 existing resource/behavior rejections. GPU1 physical-UUID binding was correct, and the GPU was released after audit. Machine evidence is under `results/quadruped/mobile_keyed_insertion_recovery/training/completion_aligned_transport/checkpoints/`, `training/completion_tail_aligned_transport/checkpoints/`, `validation/completion_projected_transport/preflight/`, `validation/completion_projected_transport/instance_01/`, and `validation/completion_projected_transport/all_instances/`.

Theory and method identity must describe the composite actor. Finite-mixture projection guarantees only per-step common-relative directional gaps on public action proxies, not safety, support, finite-time completion, or task superiority in real contact dynamics; these remain empirically checked by the shared physics contract and development gates. Actual candidate use on a positive-measure state set is necessary for transport attribution, but 76.06% physical admission cannot replace task-performance comparisons against task-only and ablations on a new independent roster. T4 is method-side ready for new shared behavioral predeployment. The paper still cannot start a new formal confirmation because T2 shared-route limitations and existing T3 frozen-contract drift require separate treatment in final protocol freezing.

T3 supports only the mechanism that late-phase coverage and explicit hold reserves can repair terminal arrival, not universal transport superiority. Fresh same-policy stress refuted the current heuristic guard's constraint-filter evidence, so the paper no longer retains that safety claim.

The wrapper prevents reference overshoot past a single goal point and clips the active-axis command to zero when reference reaches it, preventing further TCP/reference lag correction under low-gain latency. Allow bounded reference overshoot of the goal point within the declared lead envelope based on measured TCP/reference lag; admit only actions monotonically reducing terminal-set distance and passing the existing 1 ms governor. Inside the terminal set, shrink tangential residual and preserve the full 15-control dwell. Do not add hybrid force, hidden mechanism labels, method identity, or threshold/deadline relaxation.

Three shared failure classes are now localized to testable control mechanisms. First is phase-transition reference energy: when mode 0 switches at the outlet using geometry alone, TCP may still lag the reference, and backend impedance projection turns a reference jump into a force peak. Simply using measured-set transitions or fixed lead caps causes overforce or stagnation, respectively. The final change bounds lead and impedance energy before the public geometric outlet, latches endpoint arrival, and unloads reference toward measured position with a 20 mm/s ceiling. The tangential spring budget is capped at 8.8 N using 55% of the lateral-force ceiling.

Second is finite-time terminal capture: passively waiting for TCP convergence pushes route completion near the deadline, leaving no continuous 15-control hold. Mode 0 now explicitly reserves a 3.0 s completion window and uses endpoint latching to prevent boundary switching; mode 1 reserves another full dwell window beyond the existing dwell budget. Third is clearance descent/contact coupling: fixed high-speed mode-1 descent causes tail overforce on recontact, while hard stopping causes hovering, repeated contact, and timeout. The final descent ceiling is continuously multiplied by a force-release factor derived from public wrench, retaining bounded safe descent even far from the planar terminal set.

These changes support versioning the next shared T2 contract as `task_contract_v4`. Freeze the controller version, public inputs, 20 s deadline, 2 mm/2 degree terminal set, 15-control dwell, 25 N total force, 10 N lateral force, and existing mode semantics, binding the same executor to T2 main, every baseline, and every ablation. Behavioral qualification, architecture audit, CPU package audit, and GPU1 device audit must still finish before versioning; only after passage may a new GPU1-only P6 begin.

Theory must tighten three points: reachability theorems explicitly require nonempty intersection of safe feasible and deadline-positive-progress actions; finite-time bounds decompose acquisition, outlet traversal, goal capture, and dwell, replacing commanded-velocity bounds with measured closed-loop progress lower bounds; phase transitions additionally require bounded reference energy/lead and terminal unloading. Current 32/32 establishes only passage of a shared T2 benchmark-infrastructure development gate, not paper main-table performance or transport superiority. Formal conclusions still require entirely new main/baseline/ablation runs after freezing `task_contract_v4`.

### 2026-10-27 Transport causal attribution and relative-response mechanism review

T2 same-state handoff attribution is complete. All six handoffs from frozen source results reproduced within the predeclared admission domain. Three originally successful trajectories succeeded under both normal and exact-common continuation; three original timeouts remained timeouts under normal continuation, while exact-common continuation from the same augmented states succeeded in all three. All twelve continuation evidence records passed independent audit. This round therefore provides no shared-completion-defect evidence; the three failures belong to method continuation or interface handoff. Report: `results/arm/guided_contact_alignment/validation/handoff_state_attribution/gpu1_exact_state/report.json`, SHA256 `3665794ca218cfbc79d0aae017398e2fd9d3d268f4decb0cdbfc1adb7a43109d`.

The qualified T4 composite/task-only factorial comparison is complete, not merely planned. Each of four cells has 64 episodes: transport achieves 64/64 with projection both off and on; task-only achieves 44/64 with projection off and 64/64 with projection on. All 256 evidence records have zero constraint violations and execution errors and passed independent reaudit. With shared projection on, the primary transport-minus-task-only success-rate difference is 0, exact McNemar `p=1`; the old 20/64 gap came from public completion feedback. The projection-off difference cannot be described as an independent transport benefit of the deployed method. Post hoc completion time suggests transport uses 9.31 fewer controls on average, but this was not the predeclared primary metric and cannot be rewritten as a positive contribution. Report SHA256: `52409f40f760ae526f308c8b3a6fd8feadac24195ee8e57060a7bbf53a82c7ec`; independent audit: `aa9757e7295d5ebde1e662f735e338abdb0f670e660125acc2adcd0fbc5176f2`.

T3 then completed a genuine same-initialization anchoring ablation. Both branches start from identical per-instance task-only checkpoints, with identical public data, sample streams, fresh optimizers, 1500 updates, model capacities, and terminal endpoints; the only difference is response-anchoring weight 1 versus 0. Both achieve 128/128 success and zero constraint violations, with success-rate difference 0 and McNemar `p=1`. Completion times are 528.38 and 527.32 controls, making anchoring 1.06 controls slower; total-force differences are nonsignificant and lateral force shows only a small post hoc change. Report SHA256: `b92911efa1c369c4a719d8e7b058fe25ab9b0a757574c3e43d9831eeed2de65b`; independent audit: `33ce6ad720eedd7ac0f6210c7bf3e1d129b460e0578eb32c5b3aa7e2979f235e`.

CPU autograd diagnosis explains why the first ablation nearly matched task-only: response gradients were only 0.037%--0.250% of task/progress gradients across eight instances, median 0.123%. Task-only-relative response normalization was therefore added within the same `cross_ebdm` solver, without new top-level solvers, learning-algorithm directories, or P-number entry points. After normalization, the ratio is 10.8%--44.0%, median 32.2%. Eight GPU1 training instances use exactly the same starts and sample streams. In actual selector/fallback outputs, 7/8 reduce held-out recovery response discrepancy by at least 10%, median ratio 0.807, establishing that transport now contributes an identifiable training mechanism rather than a numerically vanishing regularizer.

The stronger mechanism still does not yield task gains. The newly frozen T3 comparison contains 128 paired requests: relative-response transport achieves 124/128, task-only 128/128, with zero constraint violations and execution errors. All 4 additional timeouts come from instance 03, the only instance failing the linear-progress selector and falling back. Among 124 jointly successful pairs, transport uses 5.22 more controls on average, mean reduction fraction -0.98%, with 61 faster, 61 slower, and 2 tied; one-sided exact sign-test `p=0.536`, and only 5/8 instances have nonnegative directions. Even post hoc replacement of instance 03 with fallback exactly matching the paired task-only yields at most approximately 0.58% mean speedup, 61 faster versus 49 slower pairs, `p=0.147`, still below the frozen 2% gate. Mean differences are only +0.53 controls in mode 0 and -11.35 in mode 1, indicating that the current gate does not establish the correct directional relationship between source response and target task progress. Report SHA256: `8817efdc4d8e8a5cc37993c777fb963488912bcfafcf142373dee3a08c66cc17`; independent audit of 256/256 evidence records: `fdfee6f5e38d8fe3d55905966703a7fb5ccafb823ff3b0f4fa557270300bd92b`.

Current experiments do not support “transport yields stable task gains and outperforms strong baselines.” Scaling or normalizing response loss establishes optimization participation, not conversion of response-discrepancy reduction into task utility. Continuing this core contribution requires three mechanism conditions first: start refinement from a strong task-only endpoint and use its equal-budget continuation as exact fallback; cover both modes in training and held-out selection rather than admitting mode-1 deployment using only mode-0 validation; enable anchoring only when source response has a predefined task/event-direction advantage over matched task-only. Even then, new same-start T3/T4 development gates must pass before freezing the algorithm.

The core contribution requires seed-disjoint four-environment confirmation after algorithm freezing, not any development repair above. The causal clause requires full versus no-transport ablation with identical capacity, budgets, and public feedback to achieve prespecified effect sizes, cross-instance directional consistency, statistical uncertainty reporting, and zero extra constraint violations across four environments. The comparative clause requires full to outperform all admitted strong baselines in the same-protocol main table. Only after both pass may the paper claim performance contributions. Until then, do not launch the confirmation table or present T4 public-feedback gains, post hoc timing differences, or model-response metrics as transport task superiority.

### 2026-10-31 Stopping ACP nominal diagnosis and porting new baseline sources

ACP's fixed-reference response-timescale comparison is complete: on one fixed reset per mode 0/1 and timescales 0.1/0.2 s, all four learned-policy episodes time out, while all four perfect-future sparse-label-oracle episodes using the same adapter succeed; all 8/8 physics evidence records passed audit. This rules out only simple adapter slowing as a nominal-behavior repair, not the original ACP mechanism. Together with previous first-request disagreement and closed-loop divergence, it supports stopping ACP gain scans. This port has not passed four-task nominal qualification and cannot count as a defeated strong main-table baseline. Artifact: `results/arm/obstacle_transfer/baseline/acp/diagnostics/reference_response_time/report.json`.

Following the system-identification/task-driven-adaptation direction in `brainstorm.md` §8, official ASID and AdaptSim GitHub sources are pinned under `baselines/ASID` and `baselines/AdaptSim`, with origins/commits in `requirements/baselines/sources.lock.json`. Independent solver packages are `genedynamics/solvers/single/asid` and `genedynamics/solvers/single/adaptsim`. ASID upstream provides a Fisher-information exploration objective and task-specific exploration-policy training entries, but no complete identification/policy-retraining pipeline directly usable for these four tasks and no visible license file; our package therefore independently implements the mathematical objective without copying author code. AdaptSim ports parameter-distribution updates, PyTorch branching-Q, and training loss under MIT terms. It now also includes simulated-target particle sampling, inner policy training/evaluation, replay-Q updates, and complete interaction accounting; Q is frozen at deployment, with no target-reward updates. Nine source-numerical-alignment and loop-accounting tests passed, as did a finite-loss single-step PyTorch training smoke on GPU1. Both packages currently provide method cores only: AdaptSim lacks four-task long-horizon policy training, upstream outer particle birth/removal, and nominal qualification; ASID lacks complete task-specific active exploration, parameter estimation, and policy retraining. Neither is a complete original-method reproduction or eligible for the independent main table.

TDC's official CoRL 2026 project page currently offers paper/arXiv links only, with no official GitHub code, pretrained VLA weights, or simulated-demonstration downloads. Full TDC also needs a visual policy, task-frame/mode prediction heads, demonstration generation, and manual stiffness/force settings, so a hand-written admittance controller cannot be called a TDC reproduction. Retain it as pending source/assets release; if public resources remain absent, explicitly label a paper-based reimplementation and independently account for extra visual pretraining and manual tuning budgets.

Next integrate **AdaptSim** with tasks rather than directly placing these cores in the actor main table. First declare tunable parameters and bounds within T1 source mechanisms, implementing the task-level loop “sample parameter distribution → train task policy at equal budget → obtain target closed-loop reward/features → update branching-Q,” retaining all training/target interaction ledgers. T3 enters this route only after the task reference passes source qualification in both modes, then extend to T2/T4. ASID must complete all three stages—active exploration, parameter estimation, and policy retraining—before serving as a strong comparator. Their conditions differ: ASID tests identifiable parameter shifts; AdaptSim tests task-effect-driven simulation-distribution adaptation. Count all target rewards and extra simulation training in budgets. This port changes neither the frozen public protocol, capacity-matched task-only/transport ablations, nor TDC's unintegrated status.

Task-wiring exploration provided two stopping signals, both single unsealed development preflights rather than formal nominal qualification. On nominal T1 Panda, old `PublicTeacher` mode 0 times out at 500 controls with 241.6 mm terminal error; its trajectories cannot be trusted as AdaptSim training labels. Existing `public_geometry` succeeds on Panda modes 0/1 in 411/391 controls and xArm7 modes 0/1 in 363/360 controls, establishing usable task-owned reference routes for both embodiments. On nominal T3 Panda, `public_geometry` mode 0 succeeds in 320 controls but mode 1 overforces at 20; it cannot directly become a four-task universal teacher. Upstream AdaptSim `pretrain_push.yaml` uses a one-step, 2D-action policy environment and requests 10,000 inner adaptations with 96 task samples each. Our T1/T3 use hundreds of steps and 13D execution, and T3 target mechanisms include discrete delay unsupported by the author's `apply_action`. Explicitly report task adaptations of policy trainers and budgets; a short-budget shallow clone is not the complete original method.

T1 xArm7 now has the first executable AdaptSim task-adaptation chain. Task-owned `obstacle_policy_search.py` runs two public modes in real MuJoCo mechanisms, rewards task success/time, and extracts 8D features only from public terminal errors and timing. Solver-local bounded CEM trains six-parameter path/speed policies under each parameter distribution. GPU1 branching-Q first trains on simulated targets and stays frozen at deployment. This CEM is an explicitly declared long-horizon policy-training substitute, not the author's one-step 2D NAF. The first development run, `development_smoke_928500`, finished in 46.7 seconds but duplicated public-anchor evaluation among cold-start candidates, consuming 1 of 3 candidate slots. It remains unchanged and cannot inform algorithm conclusions. Corrected independent run `results/arm/obstacle_transfer/baseline/adaptsim/training/development_distinct_candidates_928501/report.json` executed 32 physics episodes: simulated-target-stage training 5,021 controls, target execution 1,444 controls, and 1 Q update; deployment added 4,931 training and 1,529 target controls. Each of 4 training calls had 3 distinct candidates; checkpoint SHA and all ledger sums passed independent verification, and GPU1 was released.

On this single corrected development target, both modes succeed 2/2 before and after updates; mean reward falls from 0.6948 to 0.6936, mode-0 controls rise from 381 to 385, and mode-1 controls fall from 382 to 381. All four CEM trainings select the public anchor. Exploratory fixed-reset speed scans show controls of 362/360 versus 361/357 for speed multipliers 1.0/1.1 at gain 1.0, and 386/382 versus 414/381 at gain 0.7. The task is near saturation, and faster requests need not yield faster completion. A Q update from one simulated-target transition supports neither baseline performance nor convergence conclusions. The next qualification gate is not blind T1 budget expansion; first find mechanism/task conditions with trainable, verifiable improvement potential in both modes. Continuing AdaptSim requires predeclared multiparticle metatraining scale, complete task-training budget, independent source/nominal checks, and features without target truth before target freezing. T3 first needs a mode-1 reference-overforce repair or a genuine trainer independent of that reference. Treat discrete delay as fixed nuisance or a separately declared extension, not coverage by the original method.

This justifies stopping similar gain scans, not declaring the original ACP mechanism inherently unsuccessful. Official ASID/AdaptSim GitHub checkouts and pinned commits passed revalidation; the official TDC project page still has no verifiable public GitHub entry. AdaptSim now selects particles on the same target reset as required for an author-style multiparticle comparison: independently train each initial particle's task policy, select by public target reward, charge selection to the same simulation/target interaction ledger, and warm-start later training from the selected policy. All 12 ASID/AdaptSim source-numerical and loop tests passed.

Independent GPU1 development run `results/arm/obstacle_transfer/baseline/adaptsim/training/development_particle_selection_928502/report.json` was capped at 48 physics episodes, 180 s, and 2 GiB, using 68.95 s. All 48 completed; 13,877 simulation-training controls and 4,549 simulated/deployment-target controls reconciled individually, and checkpoint SHA passed independent verification. Both initial particles earned 0.6804 on the same reset, so the predefined tie-break selected index 0. Later target rewards of 0.6940 and 0.6932 use different resets and cannot be interpreted as a same-start effect. This validates multiparticle task wiring only, not nominal qualification or a main-table baseline. Current T1 improvement potential is nearly saturated; further small-budget runs would not improve attribution. Next select mechanisms with real trainable headroom in both modes, freeze full metatraining scale, and independently validate nominal behavior.

The sealed T3 directional-advantage gate's 256 physics records received another CPU paired analysis: `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/t3_directional_mode_closeout_20261031/report.json`. Among 128 same-start pairs, transport is faster in 54, task-only in 68, and 6 tie; transport is slower by 2.59375 controls on average. The 64 mode-0 pairs average 4.28125 slower, and 64 mode-1 pairs 0.90625 slower. Only 3/8 instances have positive overall means, and only 2/8 in mode 1. Mean source-task-advantage and directional-anchoring scores in the final training minibatch are 0.2383 and 0.1367; these are model proxies, not target task benefits. This gate has already been trained/evaluated and cannot be proposed as an untried new approach. The next theoretical revision must first verify same-target-state comparison of source and task-only before retraining. Offline stratification is post hoc diagnosis, not preregistered confirmation.

### 2026-10-31 AdaptSim outer-scheduling development verification

The existing independent `adaptsim` solver now includes author-style outer scheduling: train/evaluate every particle on fixed simulated meta-targets, replace the worst particle with a bounded perturbed clone of the best when a preset reward threshold is exceeded, and perform inner Q learning on independent simulated targets each round. Fourteen focused tests passed, covering Gaussian particle-perturbation numerical alignment with pinned upstream code, outer replacement, and itemized interaction accounting. After the run, inner replay was also changed to persist across outer iterations; tests establish that round 2 can reuse round-1 transitions. Earlier physics development artifacts remain unchanged and do not validate this correction.

Physical-GPU1 development artifact `results/arm/obstacle_transfer/baseline/adaptsim/training/development_population_928503/report.json` was capped at 96 physics episodes, 240 s, and 2 GiB; actual usage was 96 episodes and 148.96 s, with all ledgers and checkpoint SHAs independently verified. Simulation training used 29,451 controls, simulated targets 6,250, deployment targets 3,060, and Q updates numbered 2. Fixed-meta-target particle rewards across the two rounds were 0.6800/0.6800 and 0.6808/0.6808; deployment same-reset rewards were 0.6944/0.6944, so no replacement occurred and tie-breaking selected particle 0. Before/after deployment-update rewards were both 0.6936 but came from different resets and cannot establish no same-start effect. All candidates, failures, and interactions remain recorded; GPU1 was released.

These results show that current T1 six-parameter CEM often produces identical task behavior across simulation particles, making target rewards unable to distinguish them. More identical small-budget T1 runs would not test AdaptSim's core mechanism. Next use CPU diagnosis of candidate-policy particle disagreement and four-task improvement potential to select a task with nontrivial gradients in the source and both modes. Then freeze full metatraining, source/nominal checks, and equal-resource deployment comparisons. Without this, AdaptSim cannot enter the independent main table as a strong baseline defeated by ours.

Predeclared symmetric probes on speed coordinates were added to the original solver's CEM while retaining random candidates and itemized simulation budgets; 15 focused tests passed. Actual use was 96 episodes and 137.27 s; 32,162 simulation-training, 1,433 simulated-target, and 3,155 deployment-target controls reconciled with ledgers and checkpoint SHA; GPU1 was released.

The 6 policy trainings in `results/arm/obstacle_transfer/baseline/adaptsim/training/development_coordinate_policy_928504/report.json` produced 6 distinct policies, only 1 returning the public anchor. On the same target reset, two particles earned 0.6940 and 0.6804, with the difference arising from 383 versus 417 mode-0 controls; mode 1 used 382 for both. The preset rule selected the better particle. Subsequent deployment rewards 0.6820 and 0.6816 use different resets and cannot establish a same-start Q-update effect. This establishes distinguishable task-level training signal, not Q-network convergence, AdaptSim superiority, or four-environment nominal qualification. Next freeze more substantial simulated metatraining and seed-disjoint source/target conditions, reporting multiparticle-selection and Q-update increments separately while retaining extra target-rollout costs.

With this checkpoint fixed, same-initial-state comparison against the public anchor on 16 resets outside training produced `results/arm/obstacle_transfer/baseline/adaptsim/validation/nominal_diagnosis_928504/report.json` (SHA `8a094a47812f7857fe4319d2e76785770a5fdda5dd65d9cb44ce79213b51e7ff`). All 32/32 episodes succeeded with complete controls saved. At nominal gain 1.0, AdaptSim was one step faster in 8/8 pairs; at target gain 0.7, mode-0 differences (AdaptSim minus anchor) were +29/+28/+27/-3, and mode-1 differences 0/0/0/0. This small-budget policy has only weak nominal advantage and is not robust under actual shifts; the public anchor is not an easily defeated weak comparator. Further multiparticle metatraining is frozen in independent `adaptsim_meta_coordinate_development.json`: 42 policy trainings, 36 simulated-target rollouts, 6 deployment-target rollouts, at most 672 physics episodes/1500 seconds/GPU1 2 GiB. That multiparticle run completed on GPU1 in 939.83 seconds. `results/arm/obstacle_transfer/baseline/adaptsim/training/meta_coordinate_development_928505/report.json` has SHA `a827abd541d144043286871c02bb7c29a4e61ef906bd62e71de39150d334a4c6`; checkpoint SHA is `800b5d9b21d28c04159f7b775f0ff5770c3b966c34f7f030e070cfca1d638801`. Each of 42 policy searches evaluates 7 candidates in both modes; 36/6 simulated/deployment-target rollouts also evaluate both modes, totaling 672 physical executions, 52 Q updates, and 20 distinct policies across 42 searches. Three-particle rewards on shared simulated meta-targets across four outer rounds change from 0.7220/0.7096/0.7092 at iteration 0 to 0.7412/0.7096/0.7308 at iteration 3; iteration 2 has one best-to-worst replacement. Deployment same-reset rewards are all 0.6940, selecting tied particle 0. This outer trend does not establish target benefits from Q updates or distribution selection.

Using the new checkpoint and another out-of-training seed package, `results/arm/obstacle_transfer/baseline/adaptsim/validation/nominal_diagnosis_meta_928505/report.json` has SHA `445cea87211b56ef827c15e9749779dc57b8bc2ad9f5abd48e1673aed57063c8`; all 32/32 episodes succeed. Relative to the public anchor, nominal mode-0/mode-1 control reductions are -5/+3, identical across all four pairs in each mode. Under target gain shift, mode-0 reductions are -38/-37/-39/-39 and mode-1 +2/+2/+2/+1. This training scale produces policy diversity without robust task improvement, particularly slowing mode 0 under gain shift. Do not select the better checkpoint using two development seed sets and present it as frozen. Next redesign task-level policy capacity for nontrivial improvement and independently qualify it.

Independent ledger audit also found a recording gap. The original report remains unchanged, with a SHA-bound `candidate_ledger_audit.json` beside it: all 294 policy candidates record mean reward and total controls, but 588 candidate-mode executions lack individual outcomes; 21 candidates have nonpositive mean rewards. The 84 simulated/deployment-target episodes do have individual outcomes. Original `plan.all_failures_retained` overstates training-candidate coverage, so this run cannot supply a qualified baseline's complete failure decomposition. Subsequent code makes task adapters return each mode's result and retains them in AdaptSim candidate ledgers; 18 focused tests pass. New training must run independently under the new version, without fabricating old failure details.

ASID T1 adaptation first tests identifiability from public observations rather than calling its Fisher core a complete pipeline. Official commit `cf2cb670fb983f67cf2af156d4e17b4a690787e2` was reverified. Task-local `public_system_identifiability.json` fixes one xArm7 reset, two modes, three public speed multipliers, 20/60 controls, gain/load central differences, and a 5×5 simulation-parameter grid. CPU executes 80 simulations and 3840 controls. Full report: `results/arm/obstacle_transfer/baseline/asid/diagnostics/public_system_identifiability/report.json` (SHA `db3551bcc241340ada97e4adf93b4ed34ee7b1247e9fda758cf6988f152932c3`). The two-dimensional Jacobian is numerically full rank for these candidates, but the load column is weak. For mode 0, 60 steps, multiplier 1.0, column norms are approximately 0.0471 (gain) and 0.0060 (mass); multiplier 1.15 is nearly identical, showing that speeding up the same route adds no effective excitation. A noiseless grid exactly recovers simulated target (0.7, 0.5), but neighboring masses 0.4/0.6 differ in position by only approximately 0.48--0.61 mm across two horizons, insufficient to claim reliable noisy-target identification. This uses no target rewards and trains neither exploration nor subsequent task policy, so ASID remains ineligible for the main table. Next design exploration that truly improves weak-parameter information within public action bounds, then test identification-error effects on retrained task performance and interaction costs.

The independent main table cannot yet freeze an executable roster. Reading all four task-local `actor_bindings.json` files shows ACP and H2S2R remain historical `predeployment_rejection` entries on every task, without checkpoint sets resolvable for formal requests; later development pilots cannot automatically change the frozen list. FADA is `instances_ready` everywhere, but current target-adaptation curves and source/nominal qualification do not yet justify strong-method interpretation. AdaptSim has only single-instance T1 task adaptation and clearly slows under out-of-training gain shift; ASID has only the Fisher core and identifiability diagnosis above. Current T3 shared public reference achieves only 2/8 in high latency; early-withdrawal trials improve net success but introduce another overforce. Old `prepare_confirmation.py` v14 all-cell four-task strict-superiority gates, rejection placeholders, and existing invalid results cannot become the new paper main-table protocol. Before freezing, each external method needs explicit task-adaptation identity, independent training-instance/resource accounting, executable checkpoints, and source/nominal qualification or transparent qualified-failure conclusions. All methods must share a qualified current T3 execution contract. Main aggregation is fixed to T1--T3, with T4 reported separately as embodiment extension.

### 2026-10-31 T3 same-state directional criterion and dynamic-response event diagnosis

The existing directional gate evaluates source task advantage by inserting `response(source_features, source_commands)` into target `current/task_reference`, then comparing it with task-only predictions at target states; these proxies are not evaluated at the same state. Read-only CPU script `scripts/paper/cross_ebdm/diagnose_directional_source_state.py` uses frozen public validation states and 8 SHA-verified response/policy checkpoints, writing `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/t3_directional_source_state_20261031/report.json`. Among 2,896 recovery-enabled states, the old criterion accepts 9.08%, while predicting source commands at the same target state accepts 56.18%; mode 0 is 16.67% versus 42.40%, mode 1 2.29% versus 68.52%. There are 1,537 states accepted only by the same-state criterion and 173 only by the old criterion. Because the historical actor-manifest SHA changed, the script updates that pointer only in memory and reports both old/new SHAs; all public data and checkpoints retain individual SHA checks. This establishes substantial state confounding in the gate definition, but model-proxy decisions are not physical task gains, and increased acceptance does not directly imply stronger transport.

`PublicDirectionalResponseSpec` in the `cross_ebdm` solver adds explicit `source_task_state`. Old configurations default to `source`, preserving historical training/result semantics. New versions choose `target`, changing directional qualification only to a same-target-state counterfactual; response matching still compares source/target responses. CPU preflight of independent `finite_time_task_first_directional_same_state_anchored_preflight.json` passed: 8 instances, both modes, shared task-only starts, strict SHAs, finite gradients, zero GPU/physics calls. Report SHA: `a3e338f8bfe3b8e32e8fbc201ef8c88a9738608ad5b03526a6eae090abab8a57`. Full GPU1 training/comparison later ran as authorized; results follow below.

The dynamic-support response model's frozen validation set was stratified with a 10 μm event threshold in `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/fixture_response_event_bias_20261031/report.json`. Among 5,480 three-tick windows, mixture-model MAE on 685 real-displacement-event windows is 28.13 μm, better than zero-displacement's 37.37 μm. On 4,795 near-stationary windows, model MAE is 13.56 μm, much worse than zero-displacement's 0.36 μm, with predicted mean -11.59 μm. Similar stationary-window nonzero bias appears at 6/10 ticks. Existing `fixture_response.py` now adds an explicitly versioned event-probability head: predict event probability per horizon from public history and issued requests, train conditional displacement magnitude only on event windows, and probability-gate expected displacement. Old v1 checkpoints and objectives remain compatible. New `segmented_response_event_gpu1.json` passed preflight on the same frozen public bundle. Actual fit-split event rates at 3/6/10 ticks are 11.55%/14.46%/16.78%; CPU backpropagation on one real-data minibatch produced finite loss and gradients. Subsequent GPU1 results follow below. This is still not a substep safety certificate.

### 2026-10-31 Same-state T3 pairing and dynamic-event model results

T3 same-state directional anchored and newly matched task-only each completed 8 instances with 1500 updates per instance on GPU1. Training reports are `results/arm/keyed_insertion_recovery/training/finite_time_task_first_directional_same_state/anchored/checkpoints/report.json` (SHA `e97731c69d4c66ccdac1734290c2be3fbf4883171360651d82cb18f160307248`) and adjacent `task_only/checkpoints/report.json` (SHA `be5a87dd6b9120f9fd4c1b581251610e2e8c896503a86d09c56cfbab6a27ddb1`). Both branches share initialization, public-data summaries, eight-instance sample streams, capacity, and budgets; checkpoints passed frozen two-mode selection. Old task-only report metadata differed, so it was not borrowed as this round's causal comparator. New contract `configs/arm/keyed_insertion_recovery/validation/finite_time_task_first_directional_same_state_contrast.json` retains the old directional comparison's 16 requests, two mechanism cells, 8 independent instances, and preset criteria; plan SHA `a65991b40be742c619b323357c4a81f67f8a0583f31521fdf614c9e062854671`.

Full GPU1 development comparison `results/arm/keyed_insertion_recovery/validation/finite_time_task_first_directional_same_state_contrast/gpu1/report.json` executed 256/256 physics episodes with zero reset/execution errors; `independent_audit.json` independently recomputed all 256 evidence records successfully. Transport succeeds 52/128, matched task-only 54/128. Pairs comprise 46 joint successes, 68 joint overforce events, 8 transport-only overforce, and 6 task-only-only overforce. Transport's net success-rate difference is -1.5625 percentage points, adding 8 constraint violations; among 46 joint successes, it saves 4.30 controls on average (approximately 0.80%), one-sided sign-test $p=0.779$. The original frozen performance gate failed, precluding independent confirmation or claims of same-state-gate task improvement. All 8 transport-only extra failures have nonzero transport admission; among 68 joint failures, 38 have zero actual transport admission, so shared overforce cannot all be attributed to the new gate. The first failure admits 4 transport steps, falls back to task-only, and still overforces several steps later, suggesting that instantaneous fallback does not guarantee recoverability.

Stratification by preexisting mechanism cells does not change the primary decision: `payload_shift` transport/task-only achieve 34/64 versus 30/64, with 5 transport-only successes and 1 task-only-only success; `latency_gain_shift` achieves 18/64 versus 24/64, with corresponding counts 1 versus 7. In jointly successful requests, transport mean control differences are approximately -3.97 and -4.88. These are oppositely directed development signals; neither post hoc selection of payload as stable superiority nor removal of high-latency public-reference failures from denominators is permitted. Next separate response-model identifiability of discrete delay, early public-contact safety, and post-admission recoverability, then refreeze the protocol to test which shifts actually benefit.

Across runtime versions, these results cannot directly compare with old directional 128/128. Eight old/new task-only checkpoint `state_dict` tensors are elementwise identical, as are initial physics-state SHA and the first 30 requested actions of the first aligned request, but physical forces/poses diverge from control 30 after contact. Actor-manifest SHA changes from old `867bf9e8...` to `94fdcb9e...`, and evaluator-source SHA also changes. To distinguish version drift from same-code contact nondeterminism, the first 8 instance-01 task-only requests were replayed. `replay_reproducibility_gpu1/replay_comparison.json` (SHA `46d533ae3f2eb141e7ab2bfef0bcd36afff40ceaf3678150cdff8f72b07618ca`) verifies identical initial states, requested actions, physical trajectories, force peaks, and outcomes for all 8/8. Replay is diagnostic only and excluded from the 128-pair denominator. Next localize old/new runtime/physics changes before treating shared and extra transport overforce under one current version; increased model-gate acceptance is no longer task-gain evidence.

The current public-geometry controller was separately tested under frozen `finite_time_current_public_geometry_sentinel.json` on exactly the same 16 mechanism/mode/initial-state slots. `results/arm/keyed_insertion_recovery/validation/finite_time_current_public_geometry_sentinel/gpu1/report.json` has SHA `1bb0fbad1db7603a25ce471c0f0a6f05f8b661da9e2ef914a59e94baab72a6e8`. All 16 raw evidence records passed independent audit and all initial-state SHAs match corresponding learned task-only requests. Public control succeeds 8/8 on `payload_shift` but only 2/8 on `latency_gain_shift`; six overforce events begin in phase 0/1, with peak force approximately 1804 N. States remain finite and physically valid, not reset/runner errors. Shared safety opportunity in high latency is therefore unestablished; first audit/repair shared early contact and delayed execution, then requalify all methods under the new version. Do not retrospectively remove the cell from the frozen comparison; this shared defect also does not erase transport's 8 additional overforce events relative to matched task-only.

Tick-by-tick review of the six public overforce trajectories shows all first exceed the frozen 25 N total or 10 N lateral limits on the final control tick. Previous-tick total peaks were only 9.84--16.73 N and lateral peaks 2.63--9.05 N. At first violation, four are in phase 1 and two still phase 0; two z requests are zero and four +0.02 m/s. Total peaks are 50.4, 49.8, 97.5, 94.1, 854.0, and 1803.9 N; despite two very large peaks, their end-of-control normal-force readings are only 77.6 and -1.9 N. Current evidence cannot guarantee prevention through previous-control-frame force thresholds or phase-3 normal-speed clipping alone. Early contact geometry, delayed execution, and physics-substep force certification must be jointly addressed in a new controller version.

Further offline checking found that a public TCP-height early-withdrawal trigger of `.5965 m` hits all 16/16 trajectories, including all 10 successes, so it does not selectively identify dangerous episodes. To directly test early release of phase-0 loading followed by bounded phase-1 upward motion, independent `PublicEarlyContactMatingFeedbackPolicy` retained the old actor and executed all original 16 mechanism/mode/initial states under task-local `early_contact_public_guard_development.json`. `results/arm/keyed_insertion_recovery/validation/early_contact_public_guard_development/gpu1/report.json` has SHA `b61a05f2a87ad765097ac3b215e7b6bc338ede1e0f2e741f21adcb6f5840da13`, with 89.0 seconds and 16/16 completion. `independent_audit.json` reaudited all 16/16 evidence records successfully; all initial-state SHAs match the old sentinel. High-latency success rises from 2/8 to 5/8: 4 old overforce→success, 1 old success→overforce, and 2 persistent overforce; payload remains 8/8. All three new overforce events occur in phase 1 under pure upward requests, including a formerly successful trajectory with 37.8 N total/35.3 N lateral peaks at tick 46. Early timing can change failures but harm previous successes; this candidate cannot release as a shared safety repair, nor does net success identify learned transport effects. The development run lacked a pre-execution code snapshot; independent audit records post-run source SHAs separately, not independent confirmation. Next derive auditable protective conditions from contact geometry and measurable delayed state, first establish no new physics violations, then share the new controller across methods.

Dynamic-response v2 report `results/arm/compliant_keyed_insertion_recovery/training/segmented_response_event_gpu1/report.json` (SHA `fc43da7f1469c95da9dbb5b0b696f0d894e148d226dd6bfcca9c7640dfa541e6`) records 600 updates on the same frozen public bundle. Training oversamples event windows but corrects event-probability BCE with sampling-probability importance weights; 70 related regression tests pass. Independent CPU recomputation of fixture-x displacement over 5,480 validation windows gives old→v2 overall MAE at 3/6/10 ticks of 15.38/23.84/28.52 → 7.65/9.17/13.15 μm; stationary-window MAE 13.56/21.66/25.19 → 5.26/3.29/5.38 μm; real-event-window MAE 28.13/35.75/43.86 → 24.38/41.24/48.89 μm. Thus 6/10-tick event subgroups regress, though still beat zero-displacement prediction. Event-probability Brier scores 0.0554/0.0605/0.0652 beat constant-rate baselines 0.1094/0.1308/0.1467. Overall response prediction improves, but dynamic-task gain and substep safety remain unproven. The paper draft adds only the same-state counterfactual direction definition and event-mixture error decomposition, not development reports or performance theorems.

### 2026-10-31 Same-initial-state mechanism comparison of T3 public-reference unloading

Keeping the early-withdrawal version's fixed height trigger and upward command, the development version changes planar requests during withdrawal: use public `reference_position - tcp_position` to unload reference toward TCP over 0.08 s, covering three execution-delay steps plus one observation, with per-axis planar speed capped at 0.025 m/s. This is `PublicReferenceUnloadingMatingFeedbackPolicy`, not a Cross-EBDM learned component. Before execution, `configs/arm/keyed_insertion_recovery/validation/reference_unloading_public_guard_development.json` binds three source SHAs for controller, task runner, and script. It retains the old sentinel's 16 mechanism/mode/initial-state requests and every failure in the denominator. **Post-audit found that its 0.025 m/s limit exceeds the original public actor's 0.020 m/s planar limit; all eight high-latency episodes actually exceed that original bound. It is therefore not a strictly matched single-variable mechanism comparison and cannot attribute unloading effects.**

GPU1 development report: `results/arm/keyed_insertion_recovery/validation/reference_unloading_public_guard_development/gpu1/report.json` (SHA `8c257f0a6eb218d36da811dc79c4eb4a3a487a54c0c0b11908882e954626d414`). All 16/16 physics episodes complete, with zero execution errors in 92.96 s. Independent audit recomputes 32 old/new evidence records, confirms identical 16/16 initial-state SHAs, and verifies all three prebound sources. Among eight high-latency requests, old public sentinel has 2 successes/6 overforce; early-upward-only has 5 successes/3 overforce; reference unloading has 4 successes/2 overforce/2 mode mismatches. Same-state transitions versus old sentinel are three overforce→success, two overforce→mode mismatch, one persistent overforce, one success→overforce, and one retained success. Payload remains 8/8 success. Both mode mismatches physically reach the endpoint for requested mode 0 but end in mode 1 with approximately 4.13 N normal force, so neither is constrained success.

Mechanistically, median public planar-reference deviation at control tick 40 falls from 2.197 mm to 0.387 mm across eight high-latency pairs, but impacts and mode errors remain. Reference energy may contribute to contact trajectories, but strict single-variable attribution requires the next version matching the original bound. This candidate is explicitly rejected and cannot replace existing T3 comparisons or enter the independent main table. Further T3 work should first predict testable substep impacts/terminal modes from public pose, contact-force history, reference state, and delayed actions, then jointly test overforce and modes on initial states unused for model selection, without repeated single-height/speed/unloading-gain scans. Even when genuine failures are allowed in main-table denominators, preserve failures and version differences; mode mismatch is not completion.

The next version corrects only the action-support excess: explicit `PublicMatchedReferenceUnloadingMatingFeedbackPolicy` retains unloading timescale, withdrawal height/actions, initial states, and deadline, but uses the original 0.020 m/s per-axis planar cap. `configs/arm/keyed_insertion_recovery/validation/matched_reference_unloading_public_guard_development.json` again prebinds three sources. `results/arm/keyed_insertion_recovery/validation/matched_reference_unloading_public_guard_development/gpu1/report.json` has SHA `18d197518cbb9c9b77f80f5602e4244b2b6138dce0f12e43f80ec86fba4ee8d4`: 16/16 completed, zero execution errors, 88.48 s. Independent reaudit covers 32 old/new evidence records and identical 16/16 initial-state SHAs; actual planar-request peaks are 0.020 m/s with no episode exceeding the original cap. High latency yields 4/8 successes, 3/8 overforce, and 1/8 mode mismatch; payload stays 8/8. Versus old sentinel, transitions are three overforce→success, one overforce→mode mismatch, two persistent overforce, one success→overforce, and one retained success. At tick 40, median planar-reference deviation drops from the early no-unloading version's 2.197 mm to 0.388 mm. Strictly action-bound-matched evidence now shows that lowering visible planar-reference deviation alone does not solve high-latency substep impacts and may change mode-0 withdrawal enough to break the mode contract. This candidate is also rejected. Next specify augmented prediction/protection state and independent-initial-state gates rather than tuning unloading magnitude or speed further.

Both runs retain `source_snapshot/` and `source_snapshot_manifest.json` in their result directories, with each file SHA matching its pre-run binding. First-version working-tree source changed after implementing version 2, so first-version checks must use its archived snapshot, not current code.

### 2026-10-31 ASID T1 three-stage development chain and identification-search audit

The full ASID paper mechanism comprises Fisher-information-driven exploration, parameter identification by matching target trajectories against simulation under identical actions, and downstream policy training in the updated simulation. The authors' public repository offers directly portable Fisher objectives and exploration-training entries for its rod environment only. Independent `genedynamics/solvers/single/asid/learning` now implements bounded CEM with complete candidate ledgers, and task-local `genedynamics/tasks/transfer/obstacle_asid.py` connects T1 xArm7. Current adaptation uses a 60-control public-trajectory information objective, CEM exploration-route search, same-action target/simulation matching, and CEM downstream-route training. This is not the authors' native SAC training and lacks four-task nominal qualification.

Initial task-local `configs/arm/obstacle_transfer/training/asid_full_chain_development.json` executed 226 simulation episodes/34,360 controls, 1 target exploration, and 2 target deployments in 135.61 s. `results/arm/obstacle_transfer/baseline/asid/training/full_chain_development_3817251/report.json` SHA is `54889f67acb926dba3613e23f71f58bd3eaba3fc108d1d85adff3b6959808c14`. Target deployment succeeds in modes 0/1 at 384/383 controls, but identification returns gain 0.7143/mass 0.7358 versus simulated target 0.7/0.5. An independent CPU same-action 11×11 parameter-landscape audit spends another 121 simulations/7,260 controls, without feeding training. True parameters uniquely minimize noiseless public-trajectory error at zero, identifying first-version bias as search coverage rather than evidence of unidentifiable load.

To avoid a weak ASID parameter search, version 2 prefreezes a global 11×11 coarse grid and seeds identical CEM refinement from its optimum. ### 2026-10-31 Event-gating attribution for dynamic response

The existing fixture-response-v2 frozen development validation split underwent read-only CPU decomposition with zero physics calls. Script/results are under `results/_analysis/four_task_transfer/fixture_event_gate_decomposition_20261031/`; report SHA is `f120c33b7dc05aadce8eba55f465cdeecea80376146cd3eb4f620d4de73eaf17`. Across 5,480 windows, mean event probabilities at 3/6/10 ticks are 0.520/0.557/0.579 on true events and 0.055/0.062/0.068 near stationary. Without gating, the conditional displacement head yields stationary errors of 294/93/143 μm; probability weighting reduces them to 5.26/3.29/5.38 μm, versus approximately 0.35 μm for zero displacement. On real events, ungated conditional-amplitude MAE at 6/10 ticks is 34.10/40.11 μm, worsening to 41.24/48.89 μm with weighting; at 3 ticks, weighting improves 27.41 to 24.38 μm. Retrospectively thresholding probability at 0.5 reduces overall MAE but increases event MAE at all three horizons. An oracle using future true event labels is a nondeployable diagnostic lower bound only. Next separately report event classification, conditional amplitude, stationary false motion, and task-related miss costs on a new episode-disjoint test. Overall MAE improvement or threshold selection on old validation cannot establish dynamic-task benefit. Theory must decompose response error by event/stationary mode and explicitly include gating misclassification; ensemble spread and control-rate predictions still are not physics-substep safety upper bounds.

| Initial 0.25 pulse, then task-only | 1 | 2 | 3 | 13 |
| Initial 0.5 pulse, then task-only | 3 | 5 | 8 | 8 |
| Initial full pulse, then task-only | 2 | 3 | 5 | 11 |
| Sustained full Ours | 8 | 8 | 16 | 0 |

Relative to zero pulse, a full single pulse introduces two and avoids two overforce events; sustained Ours overforces on all 11 requests without zero-pulse overforce. The 0.25 result of 3/16 is neither a monotone dose response nor optimal-gain evidence; sample size/horizon cannot freeze a new policy. The stronger mechanism signal is **subsequent closed-loop intervention**, not first-action instantaneous risk: single-full-pulse 32-step overforce totals match zero pulse, while sustained Ours reaches 16/16. Later controllers and state evolution differ, so quantify subsequent admission counts, first divergence, accumulated substep impacts, and progress, then preregister full-episode comparisons on fresh requests. Risk gates must evaluate rolling closed-loop continuation, not certify safety from first-force thresholds or post hoc best mixture on these 16 cases. This only revises theoretical attribution, without releasing T3, filling the four-task table, or changing frozen failure denominators.
